Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 16 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Ben Snider argues that the current level of 
yields should not prevent the US equity 
market from rising further, though another 
sharp rise would likely pressure the rally 
The S&P 500 is on pace for its fourth consecutive year of 
double-digit returns despite long-term Treasury yields surging to 
their highest levels in decades. For equity investors, the natural 
question is how much of a threat higher yields pose to stock 
prices, and at what level they might cause recent equity market 
strength to reverse. Ultimately, we think that the current level 
of yields should not prevent the equity market from climbing 
higher, though a further sharp rise would pressure the rally. 
Higher rates weigh on valuations... 
Rising rates most directly weigh on equity valuations. The value 
of an equity can be viewed as a discounted stream of future 
cash flows, and higher yields increase the discount rate applied 
to those future earnings. We estimate that over 75% of the 
S&P 500’s current value reflects cash flows 10+ years in the 
future, the largest share in decades. In other words, the market 
is highly dependent on distant future profits, making valuations 
particularly sensitive to higher rates today. 
Reflecting this pressure, equity multiples have declined 
substantially as bond yields have risen. The S&P 500 forward 
P/E multiple has fallen from 23x a year ago to 19x today, a 
nearly 20% decline. Although several factors have contributed 
to this decline—including AI-related uncertainty and skepticism 
about the durability of recent earnings strength—higher rates 
have played an important role. 
However, while equity valuation multiples have fallen, 
valuations relative to bonds have remained surprisingly steady. 
The difference between the S&P 500 earnings yield (5%) and 
the real 10-year US Treasury yield (3%)—a simple proxy for the 
equity risk premium—currently stands at 230bp and has 
remained fairly stable over the past two years. This suggests 
that equity valuations have largely recalibrated to the rise in 
yields thus far. However, the steady recent relationship 
between equity valuations and interest rates also suggests that 
a further increase in yields from here would put additional 
pressure on stocks. 
...but earnings matter more... 
However, valuations are only part of the story. The continued 
appreciation of the equity market to new record highs this year 
despite rising rates and declining valuations is a testament to 
the predominance of earnings in driving stocks. S&P 500 
earnings have grown by over 30% during the past 12 months, 
more than offsetting the rates drag on valuations. The more 
critical question for equity investors is therefore whether rising 
rates will derail the market’s underlying earnings strength.  
We see three reasons for optimism. First, US corporate balance 
sheets generally appear healthy today. Most S&P 500 company 
debt carries fixed rates and long maturities, and interest 
expenses remain small relative to strong profits. The S&P 500 
interest coverage ratio exceeds 10x, close to the highest level 
on record, and interest coverage for the median S&P 500 stock 
ranks in the 70th percentile of the last 40 years.  
Second, we estimate that AI investment spending is driving 
roughly half of S&P 500 earnings growth this year, and we don’t 
expect the recent rise in interest rates to affect those spending 
plans. While debt is financing a growing share of AI spending, 
the largest companies driving this spending have particularly 
strong balance sheets and appear to be targeting returns well in 
excess of their current cost of capital.  
Third, although the Fed recently hiked its policy rate, there is 
little evidence of economic overheating that would prompt 
policymakers to tighten aggressively to slow growth. And with 
the rates market already pricing over three additional 25bp hikes 
in the coming year, the bar for policy to surprise in a hawkish 
direction is high.  
…and rate volatility matters too 
Interest rate volatility also matters for equities. Over the past 
few decades, stocks have usually been able to digest gradually 
rising rates but have struggled to digest sharp rate increases, 
typically declining alongside increases in Treasury yields greater 
than two standard deviations. This relationship has been 
consistent across investment horizons and parts of the yield 
curve. Today, a two standard deviation move in the 10-year 
yield is roughly 50bp in a month, a “speed limit” only recently 
exceeded as yields surged in late September. So, further 
increases in interest rate volatility warrant close watch. 
Some pockets of greater vulnerability 
While the broad US equity market looks reasonably resilient at 
current yield levels, some pockets of the market are more 
vulnerable. Smaller companies typically have weaker balance 
sheets and more floating-rate debt than large-cap companies, 
leaving small-cap stocks more sensitive to rising rates. Real 
estate and housing stock earnings are also particularly tied to 
the interest rate environment. Recent rotations within the 
equity market have reflected these sensitivities. Homebuilder 
stocks, for example, have lagged the S&P 500 by more than 
20pp since June, closely tracking moves in long-term yields. 
This uneven rate sensitivity has pushed correlations across US 
stocks to near record lows. While concerns about rising rates 
have weighed on many stocks, the strong balance sheets and 
earnings growth of many companies exposed to the AI boom 
have kept the aggregate S&P 500 index near its record high. As 
a result, US equity market breadth has fallen to its lowest level 
since the Dot-Com Boom.  
Not derailing equities, for now 
Looking ahead, easing rate pressures would likely broaden the 
equity market rally and propel indices to new highs. However, 
even if yields remain at current levels, stocks should continue 
to climb gradually higher, as long as the earnings outlook 
remains intact and rate volatility remains contained. 
Ben Snider, Chief US Equity Strategist    
Email: ben.snider@gs.com Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC 
Tel:  212-357-1744

Amanda Lynam sees growing risk that interest 
rates shift from a tailwind to a headwind for 
credit markets but views the headwind as 
manageable as long as growth holds up 
For much of the past few years, elevated Treasury yields, or 
risk-free rates, have been a tailwind to corporate credit, 
sparking “yield-based” demand from institutional investors 
such as pensions, insurers, and foreign entities. The ability of 
these investors to achieve their all-in yield targets—even 
though much of the “heavy lifting” has been driven by the risk-
free rate rather than credit spreads—supported demand for 
corporate credit and helped keep spreads anchored in a tight 
range. Credit spreads account for less than 15% of the all-in 
yield of the USD Investment Grade (IG) Corporate Index today, 
compared to 45% from 2019 to 2021, on average. This has 
effectively turned segments of the corporate credit market into 
a quasi-rates product. But with interest rate volatility now 
increasing, we are monitoring the risk that interest rates shift 
from a tailwind to a headwind for credit in two ways: by 
reducing investor demand and weighing on corporate 
fundamentals. 
Credit spreads now account for less than 15% of the all-in yield of 
the USD IG Corporate Index 
All-in yield for the Bloomberg USD IG Corporate Index, deconstructed 
between the spread and risk-free rate components, %

Note: Data as of September 28, 2026. 
Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs GIR. 
Rate volatility poses a risk… 
Increased rate volatility could reduce the confidence of yield-
based investors to deploy capital. This would be especially 
negative for the IG market, as a large part of the yield-based 
demand was targeted toward that universe (due to insurers’ 
rating-sensitive capital rules, for example). This flow is sizable, 
yet difficult to track. While weekly mutual fund and ETF flows 
are a closely watched, high-frequency data point for gauging 
credit market sentiment, they often do not capture flows from 
large-scale institutional allocators such as pensions, insurers, 
and sovereign wealth funds. The key signpost to watch for 
waning credit demand, in our view, is if rate volatility revisits 
the elevated levels of 2022-2023.  
Higher rates could also weigh on corporate fundamentals 
through a higher cost of debt capital and/or pressure on 
interest-sensitive business models which rely heavily on 
financing, such as those tied to housing and autos. These 
fundamental concerns are most pronounced for leveraged 
finance borrowers, who tend to have thinner financial cushions 
relative to their larger and more diversified peers. These 
borrowers also tend to rely more heavily on floating rate debt, 
which experiences a headwind from higher base rates 
immediately. Fixed rate borrowers, by contrast, experience 
higher interest costs only if/when they refinance their lower-
coupon debt. 
We also believe the fundamental improvements visible over the 
past several quarters are likely to stall somewhat for issuers in 
the broadly syndicated leveraged loan and private credit 
markets. Higher base rates are an especially unwelcome 
development for issuers aiming to refinance upcoming 
Software loan maturities—especially those formed during the 
low interest rate backdrop of 2020-2021, before the potential 
for AI-related disruption to legacy Software business models 
became a key concern. 
…though floating rate credit could benefit... 
While higher rate volatility could weigh on credit demand, 
modestly higher base rates may actually support increased 
demand for floating rate credit, such as direct lending loans 
(within private credit) or broadly syndicated loans (either 
outright or as part of structured investments), on the margin.  
The experience of the past few years in the broadly syndicated 
loan market shows how the linkage between higher base rates 
and demand can be nuanced. Notably, loan funds reported 
outflows when rates rose during the Federal Reserve’s 2022-
2023 hiking cycle, as recession concerns overshadowed higher 
loan yields. Given the magnitude and speed of the rate hiking 
cycle during 2022-2023, there was also a more pronounced 
focus on debt service and default risks.  
Incrementally higher rates in the current market backdrop are 
unlikely to generate the same concerns, in our view. Indeed, 
inflows into loan funds have accelerated since April 2026 and 
the momentum has extended into late September. Similarly, 
private credit fundraising reached a new record in 2Q26, but we 
primarily attribute this to the broad-based improvement in 
sentiment toward the asset class. 
...and growth is key 
The drivers of any rate increases are also far more important 
than the absolute levels of rates. Higher rates reflecting resilient 
growth are more easily digested by credit fundamentals. Given 
the solid state of the economy and the relatively contained 
amount of rate hikes that our economists expect, we view the 
fundamental headwind as largely manageable and unlikely to 
spark a meaningful rise in defaults above our current forecasts. 
A high bar to derail AI-related debt issuance  
All that said, given the sharp increase in yields across the curve, 
market participants are increasingly questioning whether a 
higher cost of debt capital could impact higher-rated issuers—
potentially derailing the pace of AI-related investment and, by 
extension, the AI-related debt issuance that we expect over the

next few years. We view the bar as very high for such a 
scenario, for two key reasons.  
First and foremost, we do not view today’s all-in cost of debt as 
outsized by historical standards. For example, while the 10-year 
US Treasury yield (5.30%) is the highest since 2007, the USD 
IG index-level spread of 83bp still ranks toward the tight end of 
the historical range since 2000 (and below the average of 
144bp). A similar takeaway is visible when isolating the spread 
of the USD IG Technology index. While the average spread of 
the sector has widened more than 20bp so far in 2026, the 
index-level spread measure of 91bp is below the average of 
128bp since 2000. Moreover, while longer-term ROIC metrics 
on AI investment are still widely debated, even a relatively 
conservative assumption of a 15% ROIC leaves plenty of room 
for the after-tax cost of debt to increase, while still likely 
resulting in accretive debt-financed investment opportunities.  
Second, highly rated Technology issuers have plenty of financial 
flexibility and debt capacity. For example, several AI-related 
firms—including MSFT, META, AMZN, GOOGL, NVDA, and 
TAISEM—have credit ratings in the range of AAA or AA. Such a 
high rating has become a relative rarity in the credit market, 
with only 11% of the market in that territory. Far more common 
these days is a rating in the A or BBB context—which allows for 
additional balance sheet leverage while still offering ample, 
consistent access to the USD IG debt capital markets. 
Only 11% of the market value of the USD IG Corporate Index is 
rated AAA or AA 
Market value by rating for the USD IG Bloomberg Corporate Index, $bn

Note: Data as of 2Q26. 
Source: Bloomberg, Goldman Sachs GIR. 
The significant financial flexibility of the combined AAA/AA 
universe of issuers is visible when comparing key credit metrics 
side-by-side. The interest coverage ratio for the AAA/AA cohort 
is a striking 27.7x. This compares to 7.5x for the A-rated 
universe. Net leverage tells a similar story: 0.5x for the 
AAA/AAs, compared to 2.5x for As.

As Tech-related issuance has accelerated, the share of USD IG 
issuance rated AAA/AA has climbed to the highest since 2016—
but A and BBB rated issuers still dominate the market

We expect these highly rated, AI-focused firms to utilize the 
significant debt capacity available—irrespective of a higher cost 
of debt capital—as the risk of underinvestment in AI-related 
innovation appears greater than overinvestment.

Banks 
Q: What are the implications of the recent rise in rates across the curve for banks? 
A: Higher rates impact banks through three primary channels: 1) net interest income (NII), 2) credit quality, 
and 3) mark-to-market changes in bank securities’ fair value.  
Net interest income (NII): We expect higher rates to have an overall positive impact on US banks’ NII given 
their asset-sensitive balance sheets, where asset yields reprice faster than liabilities. Higher long rates 
accelerate fixed-rate asset repricing as maturing loans and securities roll over into higher coupon 
assets. While higher short-term interest rates put upward pressure on funding costs, for most banks 
this is more than offset by the repricing of floating-rate loans and securities, as well as higher yields on 
cash held at central banks. Higher interest rates can also weigh on loan growth given that elevated 
borrowing costs adversely impact demand. However, the historical relationship between changes in rates and loan growth is 
relatively weak, with consumer spending and corporate confidence generally the larger drivers of loan demand. 
Credit quality: Higher interest rates can increase delinquency and default risks, particularly in commercial real estate (CRE), where 
maturing debt is refinanced into higher coupon debt. However, a significant deterioration in unemployment or corporate bankruptcy 
rates has historically driven meaningful increases in nonperforming loans (NPLs), neither of which has occurred so far in this cycle. 
Accordingly, we do not expect the recent increase in rates to materially impact credit quality unless it is accompanied by broader 
economic deterioration. 
Changes in securities values: Higher rates will also result in negative marks on banks’ securities portfolios, decreasing their capital 
positions. However, we expect the impact of this shift to be manageable this quarter. We estimate that marks for available-for-sale 
(AFS) portfolios will decline by ~0.6%, resulting in a ~13bps reduction to CET1 ratios and ~8bps hit to tangible common equity-to-
tangible assets (TCE/TA). Most banks should retain excess capital even after accounting for these losses. That said, the risk of 
further rate increases, alongside continued growth in bank balance sheets, could lead some banks to moderate buybacks in coming 
quarters to ensure that they maintain capital cushions. Higher rates also impact held-to-maturity (HTM) securities portfolios, which 
are larger than AFS portfolios. However, these losses do not impact a bank’s capital position and pose limited risk to the banking 
system unless material deposit outflows force banks to realize losses in their HTM book. So far, deposit flows into the banking 
industry have remained positive (+6% YoY and 1% QoQ over the last 3 months) and HTM losses remain meaningfully below 2023 
levels, reflecting both shorter securities duration across banks (the duration has fallen from 4.8 years to 3.7 years since 2023) and 
smaller overall HTM portfolios. 
Q: Does the above calculus shift at all for smaller/regional banks? 
A: Regional banks are more sensitive to interest rate movements than large-cap banks because they generally have less diversified 
revenue streams and therefore rely more heavily on spread income. By contrast, large banks typically generate a greater share of 
their revenues from capital markets and wealth management businesses, reducing their rate sensitivity. 
Q: Where do you see the most attractive investment opportunities if rates remain elevated?  
A: In a higher rate environment, we prefer banks with high-quality deposit bases as those deposits are largely operational and not 
yield-seeking and therefore face limited upward pressure from higher rates. We also favor banks positioned to benefit from higher 
long-term rates as fixed-rate assets reprice. Within our coverage, we highlight JPM and WFC.

### Page 21

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 21 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Private markets  
Q: What are the implications of the recent rise in rates across the curve for private markets? 
A: The rise in rates is generally negative for private markets, though the implications vary by asset class 
and, ultimately, the level of rates. At a high level, higher rates across the curve typically result in (1) lower 
levels of monetization and deployment activity in private equity, real estate, and the direct lending part of 
private credit (loans to sponsor-backed portfolio companies) and (2) potential headwinds to portfolio 
valuations, particularly in growth-oriented private equity assets and real estate.  
Most private equity assets are funded with floating-rate loans, so the 
short-end of the curve tends to have a larger impact on financial 
sponsors’ deployment and realization activity. When it comes to 
valuations, we believe much of this risk is hedged out, reducing the 
ultimate impact on portfolio companies’ cash flows and investment 
performance, but higher-growth companies where most of the value 
is predicated on terminal value could be more negatively impacted. 
In private real estate, the long end of the curve tends to matter more 
for both valuation and activity rates. Direct lending could also see 
slower activity rates as it is directly linked to private equity capital 
deployment. And higher borrowing costs from floating rates could 
weigh on cash flows and credit performance, though the absolute risk 
of a ~100bp move would be manageable.  
Based on what we have seen so far across both the long and the 
short end of the curve (including market expectations for future rate 
hikes), we see the slowdown in private market activity as relatively 
temporary, with rate volatility, as opposed to the currently higher absolute level of rates, driving incremental uncertainty. 
Q: If the rise in rates continues, at what point would you become more concerned about the outlook for private markets? 
A: Rates rising +/- 100bps across the curve should not be a material headwind to private markets, and we expect activity to 
resume once the level of rates stabilizes. However, if rates rise significantly more, we expect more concerns around the valuation 
of longer-duration assets, such as real estate and growth-oriented private equity. A more prolonged hit to exit activity would also 
further delay the return of capital to investors (which has been running around two years behind typical timelines), leading to lower 
fundraising outlooks. 
Q: What do you believe investors may be too concerned about, or underappreciating, about how higher yields could 
impact private markets? 
A: Although higher rates are a net negative to private markets, the absolute level of rates matters. So far, the rise in yields of 
around +/-100bps has been relatively manageable, and activity rates are likely to resume once rates volatility stabilizes. Moreover, 
credit spreads remain tight by historical standards, and economic growth (a larger driver of value across private equity) remains on 
a strong footing. We think investors are likely overestimating the fundamental damage of higher rates on alternative managers, 
leading to stock price selloffs of >20% that represent a significant overshoot relative to the low-to-mid single-digit risks to 2027 
earnings that we forecast in our base case. We note that the industry has spent the better part of the last three years operating in 
a higher rate environment, during which time fundraising, deployment, and realization activity have all experienced periods of 
pressure but continued to grow on a multi-year basis.  
We think investors are also underappreciating the robust structural tailwinds that help offset macro headwinds, including (1) the 
earnings lever of the AI capex cycle across infrastructure, private credit, and syndication businesses; (2) the highly resilient 
fundraising trends; (3) the stabilization of retail alternative channels, where private credit redemption requests have peaked and are 
beginning to moderate alongside strong momentum in non-credit vehicles; (4) potentially stronger returns from newer vintages in 
private credit/direct lending amid higher base rates (since loans are floating rate); and (5) a potential acceleration in secondary 
market activity as LP liquidity is further pushed out by slower realizations. 
Q: Where do you see the most attractive investment opportunities if rates remain near current levels or continue to rise? 
A: If rates remain at current levels or move modestly higher, we would continue to see the most attractive opportunity set in 
infrastructure, private credit, and secondaries. In infrastructure and private credit, the secular investment cycle around AI-related 
power, energy, and digital infrastructure remains one of the most significant capital deployment opportunities in private markets 
today, supporting both deployment volumes and earnings growth for managers with relevant exposure. And in secondaries, 
depressed realizations and persistent pressure on distributed to paid-in capital (DPI) continue to drive a favorable supply-demand 
backdrop, creating opportunities to acquire assets at attractive discounts to net asset value. In our coverage, we continue to favor 
STEP and TPG, whose strong fee-related earnings growth is not fully reflected in current valuations. We would also highlight APO, 
whose earnings profile remains relatively insulated from the realization cycle given the company’s credit-centric business mix, 
predominantly investment-grade fee streams, and meaningful exposure to AI-driven infrastructure and financing demand.

### Page 22

El 
Goldman Sachs Global Investment Research 22 
Top of Mind Issue 152

Christian Mueller-Glissmann and Andrea 
Ferrario argue that higher yields strengthen 
the strategic case for bond allocations 
Over the past five years, US 10-year bonds delivered the worst 
return in over a century—with real returns declining to levels 
seen only in the aftermath of WWI and WWII and in the 
1970s—as global bond yields have risen sharply toward their 
long-run average from unusually low levels during the post-
Global Financial Crisis (GFC) cycle (at 5.2%, the US 10-year 
yield is modestly above its 4.7% average over the past 250 
years). These higher starting yields should improve carry, lift 
medium- to long-term total returns, and provide a larger 
portfolio buffer against further yield increases. This argues for a 
larger strategic allocation to bonds than over the past five 
years, but we do not expect an immediate return to the 60/40 
portfolio, as strong earnings growth, sticky inflation, and capital 
competition still favor equities over bonds, at least for now. 
US bonds recently had one of the worst returns in a century 
5-year rolling returns of 10y Treasury bonds, %

Source: Haver Analytics, Robert Shiller, Goldman Sachs GIR.  
Finding the right balance 
The optimal equity/bond mix has varied materially over time, 
largely as a function of macro conditions. Since 1950, the 60/40 
portfolio has been close to optimal on average, but the optimal 
mix has shifted significantly in recent decades. From the GFC 
to the Covid-19 pandemic, the optimal equity/bond allocation 
was closer to 40/60. But over the past five years, it has shifted 
to 100% equities, as most investors would have been better 
off pairing equities with cash than holding a balanced portfolio. 
An improving tactical and strategic case for bonds… 
However, higher yields are shifting the calculus for bonds, but 
their attractiveness depends on the investment horizon. In the 
near term, monetary policy and energy prices should remain 
the key drivers of equities and bonds. Further yield increases—
particularly if driven by higher energy prices or more aggressive 
central bank tightening—would likely weigh more on equities 
than bonds, with increases above two standard deviations over 
three months historically triggering particularly large equity 
declines. Conversely, lower energy prices, continued core 
inflation normalization, and less hawkish central banks should 
help bonds stabilize into year-end and support equities.  
Over medium- to long-term horizons, higher starting yields 
should improve carry and buy-and-hold returns while also giving 
bonds better asymmetry as the potential gains from rate relief 
now exceed the likely losses from a further rise in yields. 
Accordingly, the case for higher strategic bond allocations is 
stronger now than in the past five years, and we see the 
optimal share of bonds in portfolios normalizing back toward 
the long-run optimal range of 35-55%.  
…but not quite a 60/40 balance yet 
However, we do not expect a return to the traditional 60/40 
portfolio anytime soon as strong earnings growth still favors 
equities over bonds in the near and medium term. Prospective 
global equity carry—the starting dividend yield plus subsequent 
change in earnings—has increased sharply this year, supported 
by the AI investment boom. Our equity strategists expect 
slower but still strong earnings growth next year, which should 
keep risk-adjusted equity returns attractive relative to bonds, 
even as relative returns moderate. That said, the case for 
higher bond allocations would strengthen on a 6-12m horizon if 
AI-driven earnings tailwinds fade or growth slows materially. 
For now, bonds’ diversification benefits are also likely to remain 
weaker than during the post-GFC period as equity/bond 
correlations should be less negative with less firmly anchored 
inflation. And even over a 10-year horizon, equities are likely to 
drive most of the variation across portfolio outcomes. 
Structural regimes point to different long-term return forecasts 
10y return scenarios (lhs, %) vs. optimal bond weights (rhs, %)

Q&A on US Treasury buybacks 
Q: What are Treasury buyback operations and how do they work? 
A: The Treasury conducts buyback operations generally once or twice a week by purchasing off-the-run Treasury securities 
(securities that are not the most recently issued) from primary dealers and other select market participants up to a pre-announced 
cap. While the Treasury is under no obligation to buy back any securities, it often purchases the maximum amount. The Treasury 
funds these purchases by issuing additional short-term debt, effectively removing longer-term debt from the market and replacing it 
with shorter-term debt.  
Q: Why does the Treasury conduct such operations?  
A: The purpose of buyback operations is to bolster market liquidity by establishing a “regular and predictable” opportunity for 
market participants to sell off-the-run securities. Bonds tend to become less liquid as they age, so by offering periodic opportunities 
for dealers to reduce inventories of these securities, the Treasury helps ensure sufficient liquidity and supports overall market 
functioning across the maturity spectrum.  
Q: What did the US Treasury recently announce regarding its buyback operations?  
A: On August 19, the Treasury announced that it would at least double the size of its buyback operations for longer-dated 
government debt (the 10- to 20-year and 20- to 30-year maturity buckets), from $2bn/operation to at least $4bn/operation. The 
announcement was notable given that the buyback schedule is typically set at each Quarterly Treasury Refunding—the Treasury’s 
quarterly announcement of its borrowing strategy and upcoming debt auction sizes—making a mid-quarter change to operation 
sizes unusual.  
Q: How meaningful is the announced increase in buybacks?  
A: While the expanded operation is meaningful from a long-end supply perspective—at the new upsized amount, long-end buybacks 
should offset at least 30% of gross 10-30 year issuance annually, and 45% if the Treasury uses the entire $6bn cap it has 
announced so far for each buyback operation—it is small relative to overall Treasury supply. The stock of US Treasury debt with at 
least 10 years to maturity stands at around $4tn, or around 15% of privately held debt outstanding. While the larger buybacks 
should keep that share roughly stable over the coming years following several decades of steady increases, they will likely have a 
much more marginal impact on the total debt outstanding as a share of GDP.  
The dislocation of the Treasury curve vs. a modeled fitted curve remains 
relatively contained, signaling little evidence that higher yields have 
been due to poor liquidity or an oversupply of long-end Treasuries 
US Treasury curve dislocations from model fitted values

Source: Goldman Sachs GIR. 
Q: Why did the Treasury increase the operation size? 
A: The Treasury cited liquidity support as the 
justification, pointing to the “significant volume of high-
quality offers it routinely receives in longer-dated 
buyback operations” as evidence of strong dealer 
demand to sell older securities. That said, we see little 
evidence that yields have risen due to poor liquidity or 
an oversupply of long-end Treasuries, with macro 
fundamentals the main drivers of the recent bond selloff 
(see pgs. 10-11). The more likely motivation for the 
increase in buybacks looks to be a desire to temper the 
rise in longer-term bond yields. That said, the Treasury’s 
purchase of over $4bn of long-dated bonds during each 
of the last three buyback operations has repeatedly 
fallen short of the $6bn cap—implying that it is not 
willing to purchase bonds regardless of price.     
Q: Has the increase in buybacks impacted the level of bond yields? 
A: The larger buybacks have had very little impact on the level of yields so far, for a few key reasons. First, as mentioned, the shift 
in supply is relatively small compared to the overall size of the Treasury market. Our typical rule of thumb for the impact of duration 
removal on the level of yields argues for a relatively modest impact. We estimate that the Fed’s 2011 Operation Twist program 
lowered 10-year yields by around 4bp per percent of GDP. Assuming a similar impact today, the buybacks would imply around a 1bp 
decline in 10-year yields levels. Second, as mentioned, the way in which the Treasury has conducted the operations suggests it 
remains a discerning—not indiscriminate—buyer. Third, and most importantly, removing long-end supply does little to address the 
macro fundamentals that have driven the selloff.  
Q: What else can the Treasury do if it wants to lower yields? 
A: The Treasury can potentially ease pressures on long-end bonds by reducing long-end auction sizes or doing away with 20-year 
issuance. Treasuries in other major economies, such as the UK and Japan, recently took similar actions, which had some impact—in 
Japan, it supported a reduction in long-end risk premium —but in both cases the efforts have failed to arrest the macro pressures 
that have taken long-end yields to cycle highs. Similarly, in the US, with the latest leg of the bond selloff having affected both long- 
and medium-term yields, any issuance adjustments at the long end may have only a limited impact.  
  Isabella Rosenberg, Senior US Rates Strategist    
  Email:   isabella.rosenberg@gs.com    Goldman Sachs & Co. LLC 
  Tel:    212-357-7628

Top of Mind Issue 152

Current Activity Indicator (CAI) 
GS CAIs measure the growth signal in a broad range of weekly and monthly indicators, offering an alternative to Gross 
Domestic Product (GDP). GDP is an imperfect guide to current activity: In most countries, it is only available quarterly and is 
released with a substantial delay, and its initial estimates are often heavily revised. GDP also ignores important measures of real 
activity, such as employment and the purchasing managers’ indexes (PMIs). All of these problems reduce the effectiveness of 
GDP for investment and policy decisions. Our CAIs aim to address GDP’s shortcomings and provide a timelier read on the pace 
of growth.  
For more, see our CAI page and Global Economics Comment: Technical Updates to Our Global CAIs.  
Dynamic Equilibrium Exchange Rates (DEER) 
The GSDEER framework establishes an equilibrium (or “fair”) value of the real exchange rate based on relative productivity and 
terms-of-trade differentials.  
For more, see our GSDEER page, Global Economics Paper No. 227: Finding Fair Value in EM FX, 26 January 2016, and Global 
Markets Analyst: A Look at Valuation Across G10 FX, 29 June 2017. 
Financial Conditions Index (FCI) 
GS FCIs gauge the “looseness” or “tightness” of financial conditions across the world’s major economies, incorporating 
variables that directly affect spending on domestically produced goods and services. FCIs can provide valuable information 
about the economic growth outlook and the direct and indirect effects of monetary policy on real economic activity.  
FCIs for the G10 economies are calculated as a weighted average of a policy rate, a long-term risk-free bond yield, a corporate 
credit spread, an equity price variable, and a trade-weighted exchange rate; the Euro area FCI also includes a sovereign credit 
spread. The weights mirror the effects of the financial variables on real GDP growth in our models over a one-year horizon. FCIs 
for emerging markets are calculated as a weighted average of a short-term interest rate, a long-term swap rate, a CDS spread, 
an equity price variable, a trade-weighted exchange rate, and—in economies with large foreign-currency-denominated debt 
stocks—a debt-weighted exchange rate index.  
For more, see our FCI page, Global Economics Analyst: Our New G10 Financial Conditions Indices, 20 April 2017, and Global 
Economics Analyst: Tracking EM Financial Conditions – Our New FCIs, 6 October 2017. 
Goldman Sachs Analyst Index (GSAI) 
The US GSAI is based on a monthly survey of GS equity analysts to obtain their assessments of business conditions in the 
industries they follow. The results provide timely “bottom-up” information about US economic activity to supplement and cross-
check our analysis of “top-down” data. Based on analysts’ responses, we create a diffusion index for economic activity 
comparable to the ISM’s indexes for activity in the manufacturing and nonmanufacturing sectors. 
Macro-Data Assessment Platform (MAP) 
GS MAP scores facilitate rapid interpretation of new data releases for economic indicators worldwide. MAP summarizes the 
importance of a specific data release (i.e., its historical correlation with GDP) and the degree of surprise relative to the 
consensus forecast. The sign on the degree of surprise characterizes underperformance with a negative number and 
outperformance with a positive number. Each of these two components is ranked on a scale from 0 to 5, with the MAP score 
being the product of the two, i.e., from -25 to +25. For example, a MAP score of +20 (5;+4) would indicate that the data has a 
very high correlation to GDP (5) and that it came out well above consensus expectations (+4), for a total MAP value of +20.  
Glossary of GS proprietary indices

## Document 377: US Equity Views_ AI capex, required revenues, and what's priced in the AI trade

### Page 2

An update on AI and US equities

Both stocks and investor views relating to the AI trade have been exceptionally 
volatile during the last few months. Most of the volatility has been concentrated in the 
AI infrastructure complex, but the latest earnings season also drove sharp moves in the 
hyperscalers and among software stocks. 
Below we address three of the most common AI-related questions we have received 
from clients in recent weeks: 
1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
2. What revenues are required to generate a sufficient return on recent AI 
investments? 
3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing?

1. What is the outlook for AI capex? 
The largest US hyperscalers are on track to spend $800 billion in capex in 2026 and 
consensus estimates imply they will spend $1.1 trillion in 2027. Consensus 2027 
capex estimates have been revised higher by $175 billion since the start of the Q2 
earnings season. Commentary from the hyperscalers during earnings season described a 
supply-constrained environment and large revenue backlogs, and these factors are 
driving the companies to tap both debt and equity markets to support additional 
investment spending. Based on consensus estimates, capex in 2027 is on track to reach a

### Page 3

larger share of GDP than any technological investment cycle since the railroad build-out 
in the late 1800’s. 
Our baseline forecast is that hyperscaler capex spending will increase substantially 
in 2027 but that the rate of both capex growth and upside surprises to consensus 
estimates will diminish relative to recent quarters. GS equity analysts estimate that 
hyperscaler capex will register $1.2 trillion in 2027 and $1.4 trillion in 2028. Their 
estimates are $130 billion and $100 billion above the consensus estimates, respectively, 
but nonetheless imply a deceleration in capex growth from nearly 100% in 2026 to 54% 
in 2027 and 12% in 2028.

The need for external financing and potential supply bottlenecks will likely 
constrain the rate of capex growth next year. Hyperscaler capex spending now 
exceeds cash flow from operations, which means large further growth will increasingly 
need funding via debt and equity issuance. While hyperscalers have added leverage, 
their balance sheets do not appear to be a binding constraint and their interest coverage 
ratios remain extremely healthy. Our credit strategists instead point to concentration in 
the investment grade market as the more relevant bottleneck, which will require 
additional funding from private credit and equity issuance. Potential government 
restrictions on data center construction or an intentional slowdown in frontier model 
roll-out could also act as potential constraints on spending, as could shortages of labor, 
power, memory, or other inputs. Constraints on capex represent a risk to the near-term 
earnings of AI infrastructure beneficiaries, but could help ease investor concerns about 
overbuilding.

### Page 4

Going forward, token demand growth will need to outpace declining token prices to 
support continued growth in investment spending. Frontier models are currently a 
key source of demand for hyperscaler compute. However, the rise of competitive 
open-source models has contributed to a decline in average token prices. Measures of 
frontier token demand slowed in July, simultaneous with sell-offs of many AI 
infrastructure stocks, but accelerated again in August. 
For most AI infrastructure companies, decelerating AI investment spending 
suggests decelerating EPS growth. AI infrastructure companies, and semis in 
particular, have been the clearest beneficiaries of the capex surge. Continued upward 
revisions to consensus 2027 capex estimates should support continued positive 
revisions to consensus 2027 EPS estimates for many AI infrastructure stocks, but the 
magnitude of revisions will likely diminish relative to what occurred in H1 2026.

### Page 5

2. What revenues are required to generate a return on AI investments? 
Our equity analysts estimate that the hyperscalers would need to generate annual 
AI revenues of roughly $300 billion in the next few years to break even on their 
average annual capex investments in 2026 and 2027. These estimates reflect their 
expectations for average annual AI capex in 2026 and 2027 for the US hyperscalers, 
including AMZN, GOOGL, META, MSFT, ORCL, and SPCX. The breakeven revenues 
required for this capex represent the sum of annual depreciation and operating 
expenses. Exhibit 8 provides an estimated sensitivity of required AI revenues the 
hyperscalers must generate in different return on invested capital (“ROIC”) scenarios. 
These required revenue estimates are anchored on average annual capex in 2026 and 
2027 and would argue for even larger estimates based on cumulative capex.

### Page 6

The hyperscalers’ current estimated AI revenues remain below the amount that 
would allow them to break even on their capex, but those revenues are growing 
quickly and revenue backlogs are sizable. The hyperscalers have started to monetize 
their AI investments. Year/year cloud revenue growth for AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT, and 
ORCL has accelerated from 25% in 2024 to 48% in Q2 2026. In dollar terms, annualized 
cloud revenues in excess of their 2024 growth rate registered nearly $70 billion in Q2 
and have been roughly doubling during the past few quarters. The three major US public 
cloud companies (AMZN, GOOGL, MSFT) have also announced a combined revenue 
backlog of $1.7 trillion, which provides line of sight into these companies’ ability to meet 
required revenue targets.

### Page 7

The magnitude of capex in 2026 and 2027 points to even larger “required” annual 
revenues in the next few years for the “application layer” of companies buying 
much of that compute from the hyperscalers. These revenues reflect the additional 
costs and profit margins of application companies on top of the “required” hyperscaler 
revenues selling compute. EBIT margins for large-cap software companies have 
averaged 40% during the past five years. We utilize EBIT margins rather than gross 
margins to include the costs of both training and inference, and assume additional 
expenses alongside compute. The range of potential required revenues is wide 
depending on which companies constitute the application layer and how their costs are 
structured.  
Exhibit 11 provides a range of potential “required” revenues based on hyperscaler 
ROIC and application layer EBIT margins. For example, the application layer would 
have to generate over $1 trillion in annual revenues if those companies had a 30% EBIT 
margin and the hyperscalers generated a 10%-20% return on invested capital. 
The “required” application layer revenues that would justify current capex are large 
but potentially achievable. For example, estimates of global advertising and software 
spending each equal roughly $1.5 trillion in 2026. Our economists estimate that total 
annual AI US labor productivity gains that will potentially accrue to capital total roughly 
$1.8 trillion. However, not all of these gains will necessarily accrue to the infrastructure 
and application layer companies. In addition, another year of strong capex growth in 
2028 would raise the hurdle further. AI-related revenues and capex needs would be 
substantially larger accounting for physical AI or the potential emergence of AGI.

### Page 8

3. What outlook for AI is the equity market pricing? 
While market participants express skepticism about the durability of AI 
infrastructure stocks’ earnings growth, that view is at least partially priced. The 
median AI infrastructure stock has de-rated from 32x consensus forward earnings in 
April 2026 to 22x today. In addition to tracking standard P/E multiples, comparing 
recent changes in market cap to recent changes in earnings can signal how long the 
market expects current earnings strength to persist. Among various AI infrastructure 
baskets, that ratio remains most elevated among optical networking, electrical grid, and 
liquid cooling stocks while expectations appear most conservative among 
semiconductors and utilities. 
Semiconductor valuations appear to embed particularly skeptical views regarding 
the sustainability of current earnings strength. Valuations have declined sharply 
among Semis stocks, which is typical among cyclical companies with strong near-term 
growth but uncertain medium-term growth. We believe the investment opportunity is 
more compelling in fabless chip stocks than in memory stocks. Our equity analysts are 
Buy-rated on NVDA and AVGO but Neutral-rated on MU.

### Page 9

Memory stock prices imply earnings will decline in coming years, but the degree of 
pessimism does not appear particularly extreme relative to past cycles. Memory 
stocks currently trade at a 2-year forward P/E of 4x, half their 15-year average, but 
Memory firms currently enjoy gross margins of roughly 80%, more than double their 
historical average. To bring the current P/E of memory stocks to its 15-year average, 
earnings in coming years would need to decline by roughly 50%. While this decline is 
large in absolute terms, memory is a cyclical industry and has experienced larger EPS 
declines, including outright negative earnings, during previous cycles. This pricing will 
prove too pessimistic if 2028 forecasts are realized, but many investors have expressed 
skepticism about rotating back into higher beta pockets of the infrastructure trade as 
capex growth begins to normalize.

### Page 10

In contrast, the fabless chip stocks also trade at discounted valuations but have had 
less extreme margin expansion. Margins for the largest fabless chip stocks are only 
modestly above their historical average. Earnings in the next few years would need to 
decline by about 10% to bring the P/E back to its historical average. This is similar to the 
magnitude of historical realized cyclical earnings declines for the group.

The likelihood of decelerating capex growth should be a positive catalyst for the 
hyperscalers. We previously laid out three conditions for the hyperscalers to regain 
leadership. These include decelerating macroeconomic growth, demonstrated 
monetization of AI investment, and decelerating capex growth. Our economists believe 
US economic growth is no longer accelerating. The hyperscalers have already started to 
demonstrate signs of monetization, and we expect capex growth will slow in 2027. 
Uncertainty about the length, magnitude, and return on investment for this capex cycle 
will likely persist. However, the hyperscalers have de-rated to their lowest P/E multiples 
in over a decade, and the group now trades just four turns above the median S&P 500 
company, its smallest premium on record. While investors have recently been focused 
more on the hyperscalers’ negative free cash flows than on their earnings, visibility into 
improving FCF as revenues accelerate and capex decelerates should be supportive for 
the stocks.

### Page 11

We expect the progression of the AI trade towards application roll-out will remain a 
source of return dispersion. We believe the “winners” and “losers” from the application 
layer will take several quarters to play out, as AI applications get built and adoption 
broadens. 
The threat of disruption had weighed on the share prices of many Software stocks 
earlier this year, but the group has rebounded sharply in recent weeks. Reported 
Software fundamentals have shown little sign of AI disruption, with annual recurring 
revenue growth for the median stock in GS analyst coverage accelerating slightly in 
recent quarters. A group of Software stocks has rallied by 45% and is now within 8% of 
its high reached in late 2025. Software stocks focused on IT infrastructure and 
cybersecurity have posted the strongest returns, while enterprise management software 
remain below their previous highs.

### Page 12

Consumer-facing businesses with high switching costs have also recently emerged 
as a potential area of disruption. The increasing use of agents, including by new 
consumer-facing tools such as Muse, may disrupt the business models of some 
consumer firms. Our colleagues in Global Banking & Markets developed a basket of 
“consumer inertia” stocks (GSXUSWCH) where user stickiness is driven more by effort 
required to take action rather than product differentiation. The basket includes 
insurance, telecom, travel, subscriptions, marketplaces, and fintech. The basket has 
lagged a sector-neutral hedge by 5 pp since mid-September.

### Page 13

Enterprise AI adoption remains in early stages but spending has recently 
accelerated, suggesting the impact of AI on corporate earnings should grow 
increasingly visible in coming quarters. The Census Bureau survey suggests that 40% 
of large US firms are using AI in the production of goods and services. Our analysis of Q2 
earnings commentary suggests roughly 50% of S&P 500 companies are using AI in the 
context of productivity, but very few companies have quantified the productivity uplift 
today. However, the revenue run rates of model providers and alternative measures of 
company spending on AI have accelerated during the last several months alongside the 
increasing use of agentic harnesses. 
In contrast with past periods of market exuberance, the equity market today does 
not appear to be pricing expectations of widespread productivity gains. Baskets of 
AI productivity beneficiaries have traded roughly sideways on a relative basis YTD, 
matching the trajectory of near-term forward EPS estimates. Similarly, the measure of 
market-implied long-term growth expectations in Exhibit 22 is only slightly above the 
historical average (for details, see US Equity Views: The next phases of the AI trade).

### Page 1

From:    Yardeni QuickTakes <yardeni-research@ghost.io>
Subject: WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
Date:    3 hours ago
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?

YARDENI QUICKTAKES
WEEKLY WEBCAST: Question
For The Fed: Where Is Waldo?
By Ed Yardeni • 21 Oct 2026
View in browser

The risks to the Fed’s dual mandate are constantly changing. Its attempt
to hit its inflation target is complicated by price pressures beyond the
control of interest rates, and its attempt to hit on a federal funds rate that
neither over-stimulates nor over-restricts economic growth is complicated
by the elusive nature of this neutral rate. Today, Elias and Ed examine the
forces with bearing on the Fed’s rate decisions since 2022. They also
explain why they believe that the neutral federal funds rate has risen over
recent years and the implications for monetary policy looking ahead.

《歐元投資關鍵-歐元區經濟活動加速擴張》
7月以來，北約示警灰色地帶模糊戰的風險升溫，導
致歐元短線偏弱 ，歐洲資金明顯轉向瑞郎進行避險 。
然而，歐洲基本面此時卻持續改善。歐元區9月景氣
加速擴張 ，綜合PMI初值創41個月新高 ，服務業與
製造業同步改善 ，其中製造業產出創 55個月高點。
德國成長動能增強 ，法國則10個月來首度恢復擴張 。
新訂單連續第3個月成長，增速為2022年5月以來最
快，出口訂單亦持續回升，帶動未完成訂單自2022
年6月以來首度增加。另一方面，中東衝突推升能源

價格，使投入成本與售價增速升至近4個月高點。整
體而言，歐元區經濟韌性優於預期 ，但通膨升溫與
企業信心偏弱仍是後續關注焦點。
貨幣政策方面 ，歐洲央行官員持續釋出偏鷹訊號 ，
認為能源價格再度上漲將推升並延長通膨壓力 ，未
來數月歐元區通膨可能接近4%。愛爾蘭央行行長表
示不排除10月再度升息，強調每次會議皆有行動可
能。首席經濟學家 Lane指出第 2輪能源衝擊將帶動
食品與商品價格上漲 。多位官員認為經濟仍具韌性 ，
為進一步收緊政策提供空間 ，市場也預期未來一年
歐洲央行仍有多次升息機會 ，以防止通膨預期根深
蒂固。基本面繼續支持 ECB升息的環境下 ，預計美
歐利差收斂將提供歐元支撐。4Q26目標價1.19。

4
《英鎊投資關鍵-英國央行維持利率3.75%不變》
9月經濟數據方面，9月PMI連續第3個月擴張，但成
長動能放緩。綜合PMI初值創6月以來新低，製造業
與服務業活動同步降溫 。其中，服務業受內需疲弱
及地緣政治不確定性拖累；製造業則受惠於 AI投資
與國防支出增加 ，但消費需求疲弱仍構成壓力 。通
膨壓力持續升溫，企業投入成本創6月以來最高，並
推升企業售價增幅 。英國經濟維持低速擴張 ，GDP
季增率僅約0.1%，但成本與通膨壓力升高 ，反映經
濟成長疲弱與通膨升溫並存的局面。
貨幣政策方面，9月央行維持利率3.75%不變，為成

熟國家少數維持利率不變的國家，英鎊兌美元創3個
月新低。央行總裁貝里表示 ，能源成本上升對英國
物價和薪資影響有限 ，但若這種波動持續下去 ，未
來有可能需要調高基準利率 。目前消費者物價指數
為3.1%，低於4月央行預測值 ，實際通膨展望低於
預期提供央行維持貨幣政策的基調 。展望後續，歐
非中東地緣政治情勢短期仍處於高不確定性階段 ，
並且導致近期天然氣價格飆升 ，英國仍有升息壓力 。
英國貨幣政策短線持穩 ，而經濟數據面則小幅改善 ，
但財政政策不確定性風險大幅削弱英鎊的投資誘因 ，
維持英鎊中性評等，英鎊兌美元年底目標1.34。

後大幅拉回 ，2026年7-8月份日本干預匯市共動用
15.4兆日圓，創2022年以來最高金額。8月美日聯手
干預日圓以來一度回測 160，但9月1日再度從160.4

測日本政府年金投資基金(GPIF)基金(1.85兆美元全球
最大養老基金)進場購買日本公債。同時，美國財長
貝森特示警日圓空頭交易，表示｢掌握不對稱資訊，
現在莊家是我｣。加上日本公布7月名目現金薪資年增
4.7%，創29年最大升幅，日本央行貨幣政策正常化
路徑延續。貝森特支持日本以美國公債作為抵押，讓

日本在不出售美國公債的情況下買入日圓 (FIMA-外
國與國際貨幣當局附買回機制)。目前日本持有1.1兆
美元公債，同時加上美國政府表態支持干預弱勢日圓，
美日聯手讓日圓短線貶勢暫時告一段落。美元兌日圓
9月在160再度形成一波回檔，顯示美日政府力守160
整點關卡的決心。目前日本利率(無擔保隔夜拆款利
率)1.25%水準再同時加入日圓隔夜指數交換利率隱
含升息3碼，2027年利率水準與歐美差距仍有一段距
離，實質利率仍為負值，且在成熟市場中偏低。除此

《盤勢看法》
今年以來在殖利率上升的過程中 ，美國股市展現相
當強的韌性 ，主要貢獻來自強勁的獲利成長 ，估值
則還是受到一定程度的壓縮 。另外也需留意，7至8
月走的是擴散行情 ，資金往金融 、健護及部分價值
型族群流動，但9月市場開始定價聯準會的升息之後，
市場廣度快速收斂 。利率上揚 、廣度惡化等訊號浮
現，短線震盪風險不可不防 ，長線趨勢則仍受基本
面支撐。

### Page 2

不做任何擔保，如有錯漏或疏忽，本行並不負任何法律責任。任何人因信賴此等資料而做出或改變投資決策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資之風險，並就投
資結果自行負責。未經本公司許可，本資料及訊息不得逕行抄錄、翻印或另作派發。本行以上市場資訊與分析，不涉及特定投資標的之建議。
《貨幣政策-風險管理式升息，而非典型的升息循環
重新啟動》
9月FOMC會議全票通過升息一碼至 3.75%-4.0%，
這是自2023年7月以來的首次升息 。本次會議的重
點可歸納如下：
◼ 利率預估點陣圖(dot-plot)：至2026年底前還有
一碼的升息空間 ，2027年預期維持不動(仍需留
意華許並未提交自己的利率預測)。
◼ 經濟預估 (SEP)：上調通膨與經濟成長預估值 ，
同時下調失業率 ，是一個很鷹派的組合 ，不過
調整幅度其實很小 ，2026年的整體 PCE通膨和
核心PCE通膨僅上調0.1個百分點。
◼ 核心訊息解讀與判斷：本次會議並非典型的升
息循環重新啟動 ，而是 「不承諾路徑的風險管
理式升息」。
華許對通膨的的標準不光是CPI或PCE年增率有沒有

降下來，而是通膨廣度有沒有收斂 ，他認為目前太
多子類別的價格漲幅高於3%。他承認升息解決不了
供給衝擊 ，為的是防止油價擴散至其他類別的二輪
三輪效應 。未來通膨廣度將是非常重要的追蹤指標 。
本行則認為，8月的CPI數據已經看到通膨廣度已經
有收斂態勢 ，搭配本次升息 ，預期此一趨勢將延續 。
從利率預估點陣圖，以及本行對2027年通膨走勢的
判斷，本行基準情境認為年底前再升息一碼後 ，聯
準會將進入按兵不動的觀察期。
重點摘要
• 本行基準情境認為年底前再
升息一碼後 ，聯準會將進入
按兵不動的觀察期

《總經情勢和殖利率 -推演殖利率 4Q26偏高表現、
1H27適度回落》
聯準會升息落地後 ，長債利率一度穩定下來 ，就初
步 市 場 反 應 而 言， 是 一 項 相 對 正 面 的 訊 號。 然
FOMC會議的一週之後公布的S&P Global綜合PMI
來到58.4，創下5年來最強勁水準，同時企業投入成
本價格也達到2022年10月以來最高，顯示景氣動能
強勁但通膨壓力仍存，加上財政部5年期債券標售結
果不佳，推動美國10年期公債殖利率來到5.1%，再
創近年新高。
根據歷史經驗 ，美債殖利率通常要等確認升息到頂
後才會回落 ，4Q26在鷹派論調 +期中選舉+高油價
的影響下，研判美國10年期公債殖利率維持偏高水
位。2027年隨通膨放緩，市場確認停止升息後，美

債殖利率才有辦法適度回落。

《美股盤勢-獲利成長和利率壓力拉鋸，美股短線有

聯準會升息落地後股市走揚 ，標普500指數再接近
歷史新高，不過在9/23美國10年期公債殖利率來到

今年以來在殖利率上升的過程中 ，股市展現相當強
的韌性，主要貢獻來自強勁的獲利成長 ，估值則還
是受到一定程度的壓縮 。另外也需留意，7至8月走
的是擴散行情 ，資金往金融 、健護及部分價值型族
群流動，但9月市場開始定價聯準會的升息之後，市
場廣度快速收斂 。利率上揚 、廣度惡化等訊號浮現 ，
短線震盪風險不可不防。
長線趨勢則仍受基本面支撐 ，最新公布的製造業數
據表現強勁 ，信用市場維持平靜 ，並不致出現成長

恐慌或信用恐慌 ，預料4Q26盤勢篇震盪 ，2027年
行情較為回溫 。尾端風險則是 「後續的升息不斷超
過市場預期」。

## Document 464: トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない ～投資信託の規制強化が株安を招き、実体経済や政策運営への波及が懸念される～

Source PDF: all report pdf/トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない ～投資信託の規制強化が株安を招き、実体経済や政策運営への波及が懸念される～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-09
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Asia Trends

2026 年 10 月 9 日

1/3 
トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない 
～投資信託の規制強化が株安を招き、実体経済や政策運営への波及が懸念される～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ トルコ金融市場では、投資信託による株式売却圧力の強まりを背景に混乱が続いている。政
府は国内貯蓄をリラ建て資産に誘導する政策を進めてきたが、浮動株比率の低い銘柄への投
資資金の集中や投資信託の急拡大により、価格形成の歪みや流動性リスクが高まっていた。
こうしたなか、資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）が投資信託の保有規制を厳格化したことで、株価
下落と投資信託の解約増加が悪循環に陥り、市場全体に売り圧力が波及している。金融当局
は流動性供給の拡大や投資信託への規制措置を通じて事態の収束を図っている。 
○ ９月のインフレ率は2021 年11 月以来となる30％割れとなるなど、 ディスインフレ基調が続
いているものの、利下げ観測がリラ相場の下押し圧力となり、中銀は物価安定と金融市場の
安定確保という難しい政策運営を迫られている。さらに、株式市場への信認低下に伴う外貨
や金への資金シフトに加え、金融不安の長期化が信用収縮を通じて実体経済に悪影響を及ぼ
す懸念もある。こうした影響が顕在化すれば、シムシェキ財務相が主導する経済政策への批
判が強まり、エルドアン大統領の政策姿勢が変化するリスクにも注意が必要である。

トルコ金融市場の混乱が収まらない（注１）。 背景には、現金確保を目的とする投資信託による株
式の売却圧力が強まっていることがある。 トルコでは、2023 年に財務相に返り咲きを果たしたシム
シェキ氏の下で、国内貯蓄をリラ建て資産にとどめる取り組みが進められてきた。 具体的には、2021
年にリラ相場が急落した際に導入された為替保護型預金 （ＫＫＭ：リラ安による為替下落率が預金
金利を上回った場合、その差額を政府・中銀が補填する制度）を廃止し、通常のリラ建て預金への
移行を促進した。そのうえで、株式や投資信託などにも資金を誘導する姿勢をみせた。 
同国では長らく高金利状態が続いてきたなか、企業部門に対して借り入れではなく、 株式市場を
活用して資金調達することを政府は推奨してきた。その結果、 浮動株比率が低い企業が数多く上場
することとなった。 こうした銘柄は市場で売買可能な株式数が限られるため株価が大きく変動しや
すく、価格形成に歪みが生じる事態となった。さらに、その後の投資ブームを追い風に投資信託が

注１ ９月24 日付レポート「トルコ株の混乱が映す「リラ建て資産誘導」の副作用」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666022.html）

### Page 2

2/3 
急拡大したことで、 投資家にとっては株式投資の間口が広がる一方、 リスクの集中が懸念される事
態を招いた。 具体的には、 投資信託に組み入れられている比較的流動性が低い銘柄への資金流入が
活発化し、株価上昇に伴い運用収益が向上した結果、新たな資金流入を生む循環が形成された。 
しかし、これによりリスクの集中や価格形成の歪み、市場操作の懸念が高まった。このため、規
制機関である資本市場委員会（ＳＰＫ）は８月、投資信託の純資産に対する組入比率、発行済株式
数に対する保有割合など 保有ポジショ
ンの上限を厳格化し、2026 年末までに超
過分の解消を義務 付ける 規制変更を発
表した。 具体的には、10 月末までに超過
分の３分の１、11 月末までに３分の２、
12 月末までに完全解消を求めている。 そ
の結果、 株価を押し上げてきた資金流入
の循環が逆回転し、 株価下落により投資
信託の解約請求が膨らんだ。 さらに、 複
数の資産運用会社が投資家による解約
請求に応じられないと発表する事態に
発展した。流動性の低い保有資産の売却が困難になるなか、 現金確保を目的に流動性の高い銘柄に
売却圧力が強まり、結果的に市場全体に売り圧力が広がっている（図１） 。 
金融市場の混乱を受けて、中銀は、流動性確保を目的とするレポ資金の供給額引き上げのほか、
銀行間資金市場における銀行の借入限度額も大幅に引き上げた。 ＳＰＫも、 複数の投資信託取引の
停止、 清算を命じるとともに、 関係者を刑事告発するなど早期の事態収束を目指す動きをみせてい
る。なお、トルコでは2022 年から翌23 年にかけても同様の動きがみられた。 
こうしたなか、金融市場では中銀の政策対応に注目が集まっている。９月のインフレ率は前年比
＋29.73％となり、2021 年 11 月以来とな
る 30％を下回る伸びとなったほか、 コア
インフレ率も前年比＋28.70％に鈍化し
ている （図２） 。 食料品やエネルギーなど
生活必需品を中心に物価上昇が続く一
方、 サービス物価で鈍化の動きが強まる
など、 ディスインフレ基調が続いている
ことが確認されている。こうした動きを
受けて、 中銀のカラハン総裁は先行きの
インフレについて、供給サイドの上昇圧
力が和らげばディスインフレ のペース
が加速するとの見通しを示した。 その一方、 引き締め的な金融政策を継続することが重要との考え
をみせている。

3/3 
しかし、金融市場においては、インフレ鈍化を受けた利下げ観測がリラ相場の下押し圧力となっ
ている（図３） 。通常であれば、 中銀によ
る利下げ観測は株式市場にとって追い
風になることが期待されるものの、 前述
のように逆風が止む気配はない展開が
続いている。株式市場に対する信認低下
を受けて、家計部門の貯蓄が再び外貨や
金にシフトしている可能性がある。一部
の資産運用会社は傘下にノンバンクを
有しており、 金融不安が長期化し、 規制
強化の動きが広がれば、 資金供給が制約
されて住宅や自動車などの需要に悪影
響を与えるなど実体経済を下押しすることも考えられる。実体経済への悪影響が顕在化すれば、シ
ムシェキ財務相が主導する経済運営への批判が強まり、 同氏を支持してきたエルドアン大統領が態
度を変化させるリスクにも注意が必要であろう。

## Document 476: ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd

Source PDF: all report pdf/ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか ～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低_ba60bdfd.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-10-26
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

World Trends

2026 年10 月 26 日

1/4 
ロシア中銀、２会合連続の金利据え置き、インフレ再燃で利下げは遠のくか 
～燃料供給制約と期待インフレの高止まりが重しに、低成長下でも高金利が長期化する公算～ 
 経済調査部 主席エコノミスト  西濵 徹 
（℡：050-5474-7495）

（要旨） 
○ ロシア中銀は10 月 23 日の会合で、政策金利を２会合連続で 14.00％に据え置いた。2025 年
６月から 10 会合連続で利下げを進めてきたものの、インフレ再加速の兆しを受けて警戒姿
勢を維持した。９月のインフレ率は前年比＋6.35％、コアインフレ率も同＋5.47％で、とも
に目標の４％を上回る。中銀は 2026 年末のインフレ見通しを＋7.1～7.6％に上方修正し、
2027 年の平均政策金利見通しも12.5～13.5％へ引き上げるなど利下げ余地は縮小している。 
○ インフレ再燃の主因は国内の供給制約である。ウクライナによる製油所へのドローン攻撃で
石油精製量が減り、 全土でガソリン不足と価格上昇が起きているほか、10 月の公共料金引き
上げも物価を押し上げている。原油高による輸出収入がルーブルを支える一方、国内の燃料
高は解消せず、期待インフレや企業の価格見通しは高止まりしている。中銀は、供給ショッ
クが価格設定や賃金交渉に織り込まれる二次的波及を防ぐ狙いがあるとみられる。 
○ 景気面では、2026 年の成長率見通しは＋0.5～1.0％と小幅に上方修正されるも低水準にとど
まり、2027 年は＋1.0～2.0％へ下方修正された。人手不足など供給能力の制約から、低成長
下でも物価上昇圧力が残りやすく、景気の弱さを理由にした追加利下げのハードルは高まっ
ている。財政面でも、2027 年度予算案を中銀は慎重と評価する一方、歳出拡大を可能にする
法律が成立しており、追加歳出が利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。 
○ ナビウリナ総裁は引き締め姿勢の長期維持が必要との見方を示し、2027 年には利下げ余地が
生じ得るとしつつも、 インフレが想定より高止まりする可能性にも言及した。 次回会合は12
月 18 日に予定されている。供給制約の緩和と期待インフレの低下が進めば、利下げ再開の余
地は広がるものの、供給障害が長引けば据え置きが長期化するとみられる。

【ロシア中銀は２会合連続で利下げ休止を継続、インフレへの警戒姿勢を維持】 
ロシア中央銀行は、10 月 23 日に開催した定例会合において、政策金利を２会合連続で14.00％
に据え置くことを決定した。中銀は 2025 年６月から 2026 年７月まで 10 会合連続で利下げを実施
し、政策金利を21.00％から14.00％まで引き下げてきた。しかし、９月の前回会合では11 会合ぶ

### Page 2

2/4 
りに利下げを休止しており（注１）、今回も据え置きを継続した。 
背景には、2025 年初旬を境に鈍化して
きたインフレ に再加速の兆しが出てい
ることがある （図１） 。９月のインフレ率
は前年同月比＋6.35％となり、 前月の同
＋6.33％からほぼ横ばいとなった もの
の、中銀目標 （４％） を上回る伸びで推
移しており、 過去数ヵ月は緩やかに加速
の動きを強めている。 コアインフレ率は
前年同月比＋ 5.47 ％ と 前 月 （ 同 ＋
5.37％） から伸びが加速しており、 ５月
を境に加速に転じている。 
こうした状況を踏まえて、 中銀は2026 年末のインフレ率見通しを＋7.1～7.6％と従来見通し （＋
6.0～7.0％） から上方修正している。なお、2027 年末にはインフレ率が目標の４％に回帰する見通
しを維持しているものの、その実現に必要となる 2027 年の政策金利の平均水準を 12.5～13.5％と
従来見通し（10.5～12.5％）から引き上げており、先行きの利下げ余地が縮小していると捉えられ
る。その意味では、物価上昇圧力が残るなかで金融引き締め的な状態を長く維持する姿勢を示した。

【供給制約による燃料価格の上昇と期待インフレの高止まりが金融緩和の制約要因に】 
足元でインフレが再燃している背景には、 国内における供給制約が高まっていることがある。ウ
クライナによる製油所をはじめとするエネルギー・輸送インフラへのドローン攻撃 の強化を受け、
ロシア国内では石油精製量が減少し、ガソリンや軽油などの供給が制約されている。政府は、備蓄
分のガソリン放出のほか、 規制撤廃による低品質燃料の販売許可、 軽油の輸出禁止などにより国内
供給を優先させている。 にもかかわらず、 石油製品の需給がひっ迫して全土でガソリン不足が深刻
化して価格が押し上げられているほか、10 月１日からの公共料金の引き上げも物価を押し上げて
いる。燃料高は企業の輸送費などを通じて他の財・サービスにも波及する。中銀は、一部業種にお
ける生産能力の縮小を一時的な要因と位置付けつつ、 その物価への影響が想定より長期化する可能
性を警戒している。

注１ ９月14 日付レポート「ウクライナの攻勢によるガソリン不足がロシア中銀の利下げにブレーキ」 
  （https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/655069.html）

### Page 3

3/4 
資源輸出国であるロシアにとって、原
油高の影響は複雑である。中銀は、原油
などの輸出収入の増加が最近のルーブ
ル相場を支えたと説明している（図２）。
ルーブル相場の安定は、輸入物価の抑制
要因となる。一方で、前述の通りロシア
国内においては、製油所の操業障害が石
油製品の供給を制約している。このた
め、足元のロシアにおいては、資源輸出
収入の増加 が対外収支の改善を促して
いるものの、国内の燃料高が併存する状
況に直面しており、インフレ圧力の解消は難しい状況にある。こうした事態を反映して、足元の期
待インフレ率や、 企業部門による価格見通しは高止まりしている。企業の価格設定や賃金交渉に高
いインフレが織り込まれ、 一時的な供給ショックの影響が長引くおそれがある。 今回中銀が政策金
利を据え置いた背景には、 需要の拡大を抑えながら、 こうした二次的な波及を防ぐ狙いがあると考
えられる。

【低成長の下で高金利の長期化を迫られるなど、政策運営のハードルは高まっている】 
足元の景況感は、サービス業を中心に底堅い動きが確認されるとともに、先行きの投資活動の回
復を示唆する動きもみられる （図３） 。 し
かし、 中銀が示した最新の経済成長率見
通しによれば、2026 年の経済成長率は＋
0.5 ～1.0 ％ と 従 来 見 通 し （ ＋0.0 ～
1.0％）から小幅に上方修正されたもの
の、 依然として低い伸びにとどまる。 さ
らに、2027 年も＋1.0～2.0％と従来見通
し （＋1.5～2.5％） から下方修正されて
いる。 足元では人手不足感が徐々に緩和
する兆しがみられるものの、 依然として
広範な分野で人手不足の懸念は残る。 こ
のように供給能力が制約されるなかでは、低成長のもとでも物価上昇圧力が残りやすいことから、
景気の弱さだけを理由に利下げを進めることのハードルは高まっていると考えられる。 
財政運営面では、政府が９月末に連邦議会下院（国家院）に提出した 2027 年度予算案について
（注２）、中銀は７月会合時点での想定に比べてやや慎重と評価したうえで、インフレ抑制に寄与

注２ 10 月２日付レポート「ロシア、財政運営はウクライナ戦争のさらなる長期化を鮮明に」

### Page 4

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
4/4 
するとの見方を示している。しかし、今年６月に連邦議会は予算法の改正を経ない形で歳出、債務
の拡大を可能にする法律を可決しており、 国防費をはじめとする歳出を機動的に増加させることが
可能となっている。 政府によれば、2026 年だけを対象とする特例措置としたものの、プーチン政権
を支える与党・統一ロシアは議会の３分の２を超える議席数を獲得しており、先行きもなし崩し的
に同様の対応が進む可能性には注意が必要である。このため、 追加歳出などによって財政赤字が計
画を上回れば、需要を押し上げ、利下げ余地をさらに狭める可能性がある。財政健全化の計画が着
実に実行されるか否かの行方についても、金融政策の先行きを左右することが予想される。 
会合後に記者会見に臨んだ中銀のナビウリナ総裁は、 当面の政策運営について 「長期間にわたっ
て引き締め姿勢を維持する必要がある」との見方を示したうえで、今回の決定について「据え置き
案のみを実質的に議論した」 と述べた。 先行きについて 「2027 年には利下げ余地が生まれる可能性
がある」 とする一方、 「インフレは想定に比べて高止まりするかもしれない」 と述べるなど、 その余
地が縮小しているとの認識を示す慎重姿勢をみせた。高金利の長期化は、 借り入れを通じた需要拡
大を抑える一方、 民間企業の資金調達や設備投資の負担にもなる。 供給能力の回復や生産性の向上
には投資が必要であり、 インフレ抑制を優先する政策と中長期の成長力確保との調整は難しい。 今
回の予測改定は、 物価目標への回帰に向けて、 景気回復のペースにも慎重な見方が必要になってい
ることを示すものと考えられる。 
次回の金融政策決定会合は12 月 18 日に予定されている。今後、生産能力の回復が進み、基調的
な物価上昇率と期待インフレが持続的に低下すれば、 利下げ再開の余地は広がると考えられる。一
方で、供給障害が長引き、公共料金や燃料価格の上昇が他の物価に波及すれば、据え置きが長期化
すると予想される。2027 年の平均政策金利見通しは現行の 14％を下回っており、中銀は緩和余地
を残しているものの、その時期や幅は物価動向次第である。今後の焦点は、供給制約の緩和が期待
インフレの低下につながり、 景気回復を支える利下げの余地をどこまで広げられるかにかかってい
る。 
以 上

（https://www.dlri.co.jp/report/macro/666237.html）

## Document 477: 三季度及9月宏观数据点评：增长动能边际回升，政策效果初步显现

Source PDF: all report pdf/三季度及9月宏观数据点评：增长动能边际回升，政策效果初步显现.pdf
Report source: 银泰证券

Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
宏观研究
宏观点评
增长动能边际回升，政策效果初步显现
——三季度及 9 月宏观数据点评
事件：2026 年 10 月 19 日，国家统计局发布三季度及 9 月份国内宏观经济
数据。数据显示，前三季度国内 GDP 同比增长 4.6%，其中三季度增长 4.5%；
9 月单月全国规模以上工业增加值同比实际增长 5.5%，社会消费品零售总额
同比增长 1.1%；1-9 月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同比下降 7.2%，全国
房地产开发投资同比下降 20.6%。
核心观点：
 三季度国内 GDP增长 4.5%，增速较二季度的 4.3%回升 0.2个百分点，环
比增速由 0.9%加快至 1.2%，经济增长动能边际增强。9 月单月看，出口
高增背景下，工业生产增速进一步加快；中秋假期错位使得市场需求提前
释放，社零增速有所回升；固定资产投资维持低位，但得益于政策加速落
地，制造业投资边际改善；房地产开发各项指标延续调整，房价继续体现
出企稳信号。

 9月份以来，国内一系列稳增长政策相继部署落地，其效果初步显现。国
家发展改革委在 10 月 19 日新闻发布会上表示，国内经济稳定回升的基
础和势头偏弱，后期将进一步增强存量政策实施效能，协同推进“十五五”
规划、“六张网”实施方案重大工程项目开工建设，同时及时推出务实管
用的增量政策。若相关政策得到较好贯彻，四季度国内经济料仍有改善空
间，叠加上年同期低基数，全年 GDP增长目标有望顺利实现。
 风险提示：出口增速明显放缓；政策进度不及预期。
2026 年 10 月 20 日

1、三季度经济 GDP 增速好于预期
根据国家统计局发布的数据，2026 年前三季度
国内GDP累计增速为4.6%，其中三季度增长4.5%，
好于市场预期。前三季度国内 4.6%的 GDP 增速仍处
于年初设定的4.5%-5.0%目标区间内，鉴于近期稳增
长政策力度提升，四季度国内经济仍有改善空间，叠
加上年同期低基数，全年增长目标有望顺利实现。
单季度看，三季度 GDP 增速较二季度的 4.3%回
升0.2个百分点，环比增速由0.9%加快至1.2%，经
济增长动能边际企稳。分产业看，第一产业增加值同
比增长3.6%，对经济增长的贡献率为6.3%；第二产
业增加值同比增长 3.4%，对经济增长的贡献率为
27.2%；第三产业增加值同比增长5.2%，对经济增长
的贡献率为66.5%。
图表 1 国内 GDP 增速（%） 图表 2 三大产业对经济增长的贡献率（%）
数据来源：同花顺 iFinD、银泰证券研究所

4、固投增速维持低位，制造业投资降幅收窄
1—9 月份，全国固定资产投资（不含农户）同
比下降 7.2%，降幅较上月持平，其中，民间固定资
产投资同比下降 10.4%，较上月降幅扩大 0.3个百分
点。从环比看，9 月份固定资产投资（不含农户）增
长 0.07%，自 3月份以来首次转正。

### Page 9

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
分领域看，1-9月制造业投资同比下降 2.1%，基
础设施投资下降 4.2%，降幅分别较上月收窄 0.2 和
扩大 0.2个百分点。制造业投资边际改善，背后预计
与 9 月份以来专项债、政策性金融工具加速落地有
关。分行业看，政策支撑叠加行业高景气度，高技术
制造业固定资产投资增速进一步加快至 4%（上月为
+3.5%），其中，电子电路制造业投资增长 58.7%，
持续保持高速增长；锂离子电池制造业投资增长
20.9%，增速加快 0.3 个百分点；集成电路制造业投
资增长 14.2%，增速加快 2.2个百分点；电子专用材
料制造业投资增长 9.5%，增速加快 1.0个百分点。
传统周期行业与基建领域，金属制品业下降
10.1%（上月为-8.9%）、专用设备制造业下降 8.9%
（上月为-9.0%），道路运输业下降 9.9%（上月为
-8.0%）、水利管理业下降 14.4%（上月为-12.0%）。

5、房地产市场价格继续显现积极信号
房地产市场方面，9月房地产开发各项指标延续
调整，投资、新开工、竣工降幅均有所扩大，销售面
积、销售金额降幅亦同步走阔。相对积极的是，房价
继续体现出企稳信号，9 月一线城市新建商品住宅、
二手住宅销售价格环比分别上涨 0.1%和 0.2%，二线
城市新房环比由降转平，二、三线城市环比降幅总体
收窄。
具体看，1—9 月份，全国房地产开发投资同比
下降20.6%，降幅较1-8月的-19.9%扩大0.7个百分
点。房屋施工面积同比-12.8%（上月为-12.8%），房
屋新开工面积-24.9%（上月为-24.8%），房屋竣工面
积-25.1%（上月为-23.7%）。
销售方面，1—9 月份，新建商品房销售面积同

### Page 11

请务必阅读正文之后的免责条款部分 研究专业创新 如意投资随心
比-13.2%（上月为-12.1%），销售额-13.9%（上月为
-13.0%），降幅较上月同样呈小幅扩大趋势。根据住
房和城乡建设部数据，1—9 月份，二手房交易网签
面积，同比增长10.7%，增速较上月扩大0.1个百分
点，二手房交易改善趋势延续。
价格方面，根据国家统计局公布的数据，9 月份
70个大中城市新建商品房价格环比-0.1%，跌幅较上
月进一步收窄，其中，一线城市新建商品住宅销售价
格环比上涨 0.1%，涨幅与上月相同，自 1 月份以来
连续8 个月处于持平或上涨态势。二线城市环比由上
月下降 0.1%转为持平；三线城市环比下降 0.2%，降
幅与上月相同。70 个大中城市中，新建商品住宅销
售价格环比上涨城市有17个，比上月增加2 个。
二手房价格方面，9 月份 70 个大中城市新建商
品房价格环比-0.3%，跌幅较上月一致。其中，一线
城市二手住宅销售价格环比上涨 0.2%，涨幅比上月
扩大0.1个百分点，今年三月份以来连续6个月环比

上涨；二线城市环比下降0.2%，降幅比上月收窄0.1
个百分点；三线城市环比下降 0.3%，降幅与上月相
同。70 个大中城市中，二手住宅销售价格环比上涨
城市有9个，比上月增加6 个。

### Page 1

十月 20, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 1

動態更新  軟體  ‧美國

Amazon 
(AMZN.O/AMZN US)

3Q26 財報前瞻：AWS 持續加速成長，惟買方投資人預期已堆高 
重要訊息  
Amazo n 預計於台灣時間 10/30 凌晨公 布 3Q26 財報。 
評論及分析  
Holiday  Season 線上銷售 年增率 預期持平。根據 Adobe  Analytics 預估美
國 2026 購物旺季(11 -12 月)線上銷售額將達 US$275bn 年增 6.7% vs. 2025
年增 6.8% 大致持平，係來自於全品項折扣幅度約 18-30%與 2025 相當，AI
連結導購至網站年增 130% 。4M26 起徵收 3.5% 燃油及物流附加費，禁止
Meta Muse 或未與公司充分 溝通的 Agent 服務取得 網站資料，Amazon Ads
與 ChatGPT 試行對話式廣告投放，皆為應對高油價及 AI 變革之措施。我們
正向看待電商業務對 3Q26 財報/4Q26 財測為正向助益 。 
AWS 持續加速 成長 ，惟 買方投資人 預期已堆高 。 根據 ： (1) 媒體揭露
Anthropic  2Q/3Q26 營收 US$11.6/16.8bn ，我們以推論 +訓練算力及通路費
用加總佔比營收 40-50%計算，AWS 為 Anthropic 主要算力供應商及銷售渠
道(佔比預估 60%)； (2)GPT -6 獲得正向回饋並已 在 Bedrock 上架，OpenAI
與 AWS 合作逐漸緊密，惟 2GW Trn 採用將於 2027 上線；(3)標準化算力價
格 持續回升尤其在 Blackwell 系 列更為明顯， EC2 部分服務於 7/1 調漲
20% ，10/7 將再調漲 15%；(4)管理層股東信指出 AWS 若直接外售 自研晶
片或硬體系統將增加 US$30bn 潛在營收。 我們預期 AWS 營收年增率於
2H26-2027 持續加速成長並優於賣方市場共識，惟我們觀察到買方投資人對
2H26 成長預期已達 40%+ 與賣方市場共識出現落差。  
資本支出 同時支 應 AI Labs  / Enterprise  / Rufus 成長 。管理層先前指出
4Q25/2025 新增算力 1.2/3.9GW ，2027 年底算力容量翻倍，我們估算
2027 AWS Trn/NVDA VR 每 GW 建置成本 US$25- 30bn/US$55 -60bn ，AWS
每 GW 平均建置成本約 US$40-45bn 下緣，AWS 資本支出佔總資本支出
90%(其於為物流基礎設施 )，我們預期 2027 資本支出 US$300-330bn，故
市場共識將再上修約 11%，對 FCF 而言為龐大負擔。  
投資建議  
我們維持 Amazon 「增加持股」建議 並調升 目標價 340 美元，係以 2027 年
GAAP 每股盈餘 32 倍換算 。短期 Muse 雜音/ 回檔將創造長線布局良機。  
投資風險  
市場競爭；反壟斷議題 ；企業 IT 支出或消費動能 趨緩。 
增加持股‧維持

商城 (38%) 、實體商店( 3%)、第三方賣家服務(2 4%) 、會員費
收入(7%) 、AWS(1 8%)、廣告服務 (10%)、其他(1%)。

### Page 6

美國 Amazon

十月 20, 2026 研究網站: https://investment.kgisia.com.tw/Portal/Report/Index/Zh/R 6

項目 定義 
能源使用 
直接與間接能源消耗總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司運營範圍內消耗之能源總量 
- 能源使用總量 = 直接能源消耗總量 + 間接能源消耗量 
- 總能源使用量包括購買的能源、生產的能源 
- 就公用事業而言，輸電/電網損耗為其業務活動之一部分，被視為總能源消耗，數據不包括為滿足能源使用而生產的電力（公用
事業為出售而生產） 
- 就公用事業而言，用於能源生產的煤、天然氣或核能等原料不屬於「總能源使用」項下 
購買之再生能源 
Primary 再生能源購買總量(十億焦耳) 
- 公司各種來源之能源消耗量與購買的能源中屬於自然界可再生者（太陽能、風能、水能、生物質能、地熱能）之量  
- 如無證據顯示再生能源由公司生產，則所報告的能源數據視為購買的再生能源 
再生能源使用率 再生能源占總能源消耗量之比例 
CO2 約當排放量 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量(公噸) 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
CO2 約當排放量營收比 
直接 CO2 與 CO2 約當排放量（公噸）銷售（百萬元）比 
- 公司擁有或控制的來源的直接排放量（範圍 1 排放量） 
- 相關氣體：二氧化碳 (CO2)、甲烷 (CH4)、一氧化二氮 (N2O)、氫氟碳化物 (HFCS)、全氟化合物 (PFCS)、六氟化硫 (SF6)、三氟化氮 
(NF3) 
廢棄物總量 
廢棄物總量(公噸) 
- 總廢棄物 = 無害廢棄物 + 有害廢棄物 
- 僅考慮固體廢棄物，但如液體廢棄物以公噸為單位呈報，則會將其加入求得包含液體廢棄物之總量 
- 對於採礦、石油與天然氣等行業，tailings、廢石、煤、飛灰等廢棄物亦考慮在內 
廢棄物回收率 
公司呈報的廢棄物回收率 
- 廢棄物回收率 = 廢棄物回收量/總廢棄物*100 
- 廢棄物轉化為能源或經由廢棄物焚燒產生能源視為廢棄物回收 
- 經由堆肥回收的廢棄物視為回收之廢棄物 
總取水量 
總取水量(立方公尺) 
- 由呈報組織直接或經水公司等中介機構從任何水源抽取的總水量 
- 水井、城鎮/公用事業/市政用水、河水、地表水等不同之水源均予以考慮 
環保支出 環保支出總金額 - 所有用於環境保護，或防止、減少、控制環境因素、影響、危害的投資與支出，亦包括處置、處理、衛生、清理支出 
員工流動率 
員工流動率 
- 包括任何原因（自願或非自願）離開公司的員工，如辭職、退休、自然離職/死亡、醫療失能、冗員、裁員、重組、解僱、裁減
或定期合約到期 
- 員工流動率 =（離開之員工/平均員工人數）*100 
- 平均員工人數 =（本年末員工人數 + 去年末員工人數）/2 
- 本財年末員工數 =上個財年末員工數 + 新員工數 - 離開之員工數 
女性經理 
女性經理百分比 
- 女性經理占公司經理的百分比 
- 如有不同階層之百分比，如最高階、高階、中階、初階，則會考慮中階女性經理之百分比 
- 女性經理百分比 = 女性經理人數/經理總數*100 
女性員工 
女性員工百分比 
- 女性員工占公司員工總數的百分比 
- 女性員工百分比 = 女性人數/員工總數*100 
教育訓練總時數 
所有員工教育訓練總時數 
- 僅考慮員工教育訓練時數 
- 包括一般員工所有類型的教育訓練（如健康與安全、環境、急難事件救援、技能與職業發展） 
- 如果數據以天為單位，則乘以 8，係假設 1 天 =  8 小時工作 
每位員工教育訓練時數 平均每年每位員工總教育訓練時數 
股東治理分數 衡量公司用以反收購工具的有效性 
公司治理分數 衡量公司對最佳治理原則的承諾和有效性 
產品責任分數 衡量公司生產優質產品和服務的能力，且產品是否將客戶的健康、安全、整合性和數據隱私進行綜合考量 
社區關係分數 衡量公司對成為優良公民、保護公眾健康和尊重商業道德的承諾 
勞動力分數 衡量公司在員工工作滿意度、健康、工作場所的安全、多樣性、平等以及員工發展機會方面的成效 
資源使用指標 衡量公司在原物料、能源或水的使用效率，以及是否通過改進供應鏈來尋求更俱生態效率的解決方案

## Document 578: 地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党

Source PDF: all report pdf/地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党.pdf
Report source: MURC
Date: 2026-09-24
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

2026 年 9 月 24 日

MURC Focus

地方選で大敗が続くドイツ与党

～ドイツの政局不安がユーロ売り要因となる可能性～

調査部 主任研究員 土田 陽介

1. 地方選での大敗が相次いだ与党 
ドイツの地方選で中道右派の与党・キリスト教民主同盟（CDU）の大敗が相次いでいる。9 月 6 日のザクセン・
アンハルト州に続き、9 月 20 日のベルリン州（特別州）と メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州 の州議会選挙で、
CDU は得票率を大幅に減らした。特に メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン 州議会選挙では、 CDU の得票率が
4.9%にとどまり、5%阻止条項（得票率 5%未満の政党は議席を保有できない決まり）に従い議席を失った。

他方で、右派政党「ドイツのための選択肢」（ AfD）が勝利を重ねている（図表 1）。ザクセン・アンハルト州での
得票率は 43.8%（前回 20.8%）、ベルリン州は 16.3%（同 9.1%）、メクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州は 38.2%
（同 16.7%）といずれも得票率を上昇させた。ただしベルリン州の場合は、左派の左翼党（Die Linke） が得票率
25.7%（同 12.3%）で第一党となり、AfD の党勢を上回っている。 
図表 1. CDU と AfD の得票率の比較 図表 2. 主要政党の支持率

（注）連邦議会は 2025 年総選挙での得票率。 
（出所） 各種資料

（注）FDP は自民党の略称。残りの政党の略称は本文中に明記した。 
（出所）INSA

（年、月）
（%）
〇ドイツでは中道政党への支持が後退する一方、右派と左派への支持が上昇し、政治がいわば“二極化”の様
相を呈している。 その大きな要因の一つに、エネルギー価格の高騰があると考えられる。 
〇2027年に予定されている旧西ドイツの5州での州議会選挙でも右派政党 AfD の勢力が伸長すれば、政権を
担う Union の右派回帰に伴う大連立の崩壊の可能性も高まる。 
〇2027年には4月と5月にフランスの大統領選挙も予定されている。フランスとドイツというEU の両大国での政
局が不安定となれば、ユーロ売りが進むだろう。

### Page 2

有権者の主要政党支持率調査（INSA）を確認しても、CDU と姉妹政党のキリスト教社会同盟（CSU）で構成さ
れる同盟（Union）の支持率低下と AfD の支持率上昇が顕著である（図表 2）。対して、Union と大連立を組む社
民党（SPD）の支持率も低迷している。代わって、環境政党である同盟 90／緑の党（B90/Gr）や、左派党と立場
が近いザーラ・ワーゲンクネヒト同盟（BSW）の支持率は上昇している。

こうしたことから、ドイツでは 中道政党への支持が後退 する一方、右派と左派への支持が上昇し 、政治がいわ
ば“二極化”の様相を呈していることが読み取れる。なお AfD に関しては、いずれの政党も現状では連立協議を
拒絶している状況である。ただしメクレンブルク・フォアポンメルン州の場合、AfD が今回の州選挙で 32 議席（定
数 71 議席）を獲得しているため、他党との協議次第では AfD が政権を獲得できる可能性がある。

2. エネルギー価格の急騰が与党離れに 
CDU や SPD の人気凋落の大きな要因の一つに、エネルギー価格の高騰があると考えられる（図表3）。ドイツ
のエネルギー価格は今年 2 月に生じたイラン発のエネルギーショックを受けて急騰。その後は停戦交渉の開始を
受けて落ち着くかに見えたが、結局は停戦交渉が決裂したため反発した。特にガソリンなど化石燃料の価格上昇
は著しく、ドイツ国民の生活を直撃している。

こうした環境の下で問われているのが、かつてドイツに安価なエネルギーを供給していたロシアとの距離感であ
る。AfD はロシアと近しく、支持者の中にもロシアとの関係改善を図るべきだという機運が強い。一方、AfD 以外
の政党の支持者は、引き続きロシアを脅威とみなす傾向が強い（図表4）。実際、AfD 以外の主要政党はロシアと
の関係改善には基本的に慎重な姿勢である。

図表 3. ドイツのエネルギー価格（消費者物価） 図表 4. 対ロシア意識調査（9 月 3 日時点）

（注）各種燃料、電気料金等が含まれる。 
（出所）EU 統計局（ユーロスタット）

（出所）ARD-Deutschland survey

（年、月）

（%）
とても大きな脅威/脅威 小さな脅威/脅威ではない

### Page 3

それどころか、フリードリヒ・メルツ首相が率いる中道の大連立政権（Union と SPD）は、ロシアの脅威を念頭に
防衛体制の強化に努めている。その反面でメルツ政権は、CDU 所属のカテリーナ・ライヒェ連邦経済・エネルギ
ー大臣と SPD のカールステン・シュナイダー環境大臣の対立もあり、エネルギー供給安定化のための有効な手
立てを打てていない。こうした不手際が足元の与党離れにつながっていると考えられる。

不法移民対策への不満も CDU や SPD の人気凋落につながっていると指摘される。とはいえ、メルツ政権は
隣接する 9 カ国との間での国境検査の厳格化といった水際対策のみならず、SPD が 2024 年 6 月に導入した
「ターボ帰化」制度（特定技能を持つ一部の移民に最短 3 年で帰化を容認する制度）を廃止するなど、不法移民
対策を推し進めているため、直近の CDU や SPD の人気凋落の主因とは言えないだろう。

3. 政局不安定を嫌気し始めたユーロ相場 
ドイツの次期の総選挙は 2029 年 2 月までに行われるため、州議会選挙の結果が直ちにドイツ国政を揺るがす
ことはない。一方、ドイツでは、2027 年に AfD の勢力がまだ限定的な旧西ドイツの 5 州で州議会選挙が予定さ
れている（図表 5）。これらの州選挙でも AfD の勢力伸長が確認されれば、政権を担う Union の右派回帰に伴う
大連立の崩壊などの可能性も高まる。

こうした政局の不安定さが、底堅かったユーロ相場の変調をもたらす可能性がある。 ベルリン州とメクレンブル
ク・フォアポンメルン州の州議会選挙の翌日である 9 月 21 日の為替市場では、ユーロの対ドル相場が終値で 1
ユーロ＝1.1464 ドルと、前日比 0.17%安となった。欧州中銀（ECB）による利上げ観測などからユーロ相場は底
堅く、9 月 20 日の州議会選挙の結果の影響は限定的だったとみられる。

### Page 4

一方、イラン情勢の緊迫化に伴う“有事のドル買い”が再開したこともあり、ユーロ相場の堅調さは徐々に弱まっ
ている。2027 年 4 月 18 日と 5 月 2 日にはフランスの国政選挙（大統領選挙）が予定されており、波乱が予想さ
れている。こうした中で、旧西ドイツ諸州の州議会選挙でも CDU や SPD の退潮が明らかとなれば、フランスとド
イツという欧州連合（EU）の両大国の政局が不安定となり、ユーロ売りが進むだろう。

## Document 592: 宏观周报：按兵不动背后是再加息风险

宏观周报 : 按兵不动背后是再加息风险
于洁
2026 年 10 月 25 日

### Page 3

p 3
要 点
一、海外宏观：按兵不动背后是再加息风险
CME FedWatch最新数据显示，市场预计美联储在10月28日议息会议上维持利率不变的概率为90%，加息25个基点的概率为10%；但对后续政策的预期明显偏鹰，
12月会议加息至4.00%—4.25%的概率已达68.9%。本次会议的基准情景是维持利率不变，但货币政策并未转向宽松，市场交易逻辑正从降息预期转向再加息风险。通胀黏
性、能源价格上涨及经济韧性使美联储有必要维持紧缩立场 ，但考虑到经济增长和就业市场面临的压力，美联储也可能选择暂缓行动、继续观察数据。因此，市场关注
的重点不仅是本次利率决议，更在于美联储是否释放12月加息信号；若政策表态偏鹰，美债收益率和美元可能获得支撑，黄金等利率敏感资产则面临压力。
近期美债收益率呈现冲高回落 、但仍处于高位震荡的特征 。近期美债市场波动加剧 ，10月22日受科技股调整、避险需求上升等因素影响 ，10年期收益率单日大幅
下行，23日又因油价上涨、通胀担忧升温而反弹至5%左右。当前美债市场反映的是避险需求与通胀、货币政策收紧预期之间的博弈：短期风险偏好下降可以推动资金流

入美债，但油价上涨、通胀黏性及后续加息风险，又限制了收益率下行空间。尽管市场普遍预期美联储10月会议维持利率不变，但这并不意味着货币政策转向宽松。后
续应重点关注美联储对12月政策的指引、能源价格走势及长端美债供给和财政风险。若加息预期进一步升温，美债收益率仍有上行压力。
二、国内宏观：出口超预期支撑GDP，内生动能依然偏弱
前三季度GDP同比增长4.6%，三季度回升至4.5%，实现政府工作报告4.5%—5%的预期目标区间。分产业看，第三产业增长5.2%，是主要拉动；第二产业增长 3.8%，
其中建筑业受地产拖累明显；第一产业增长 3.7%。从支撑结构看，4.6%的增速主要靠三块力量托底：一是出口超预期高增 ，外需成为最大稳定器。前三季度货物出口人
民币计增长16.4%，9月单月出口增长29.6%；机电产品出口增长23.7%，占出口总额比重达64.3%，AI产业链和高端制造出海持续验证景气度。贸易顺差维持高位，净出口
对GDP形成明显正贡献，在国内投资和消费偏弱的背景下，外需有效对冲了内需不足。

二是新动能产业逆势高增，工业内部结构分化但总量有支撑。前三季度规上工业增加值增长5.3%，其中高技术制造业增长14.3%、装备制造业增长10.2%，分别快于
整体工业9.0和4.9个百分点；3D打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产量分别增长62.5%、39.5%、29.1%。信息传输、软件和信息技术服务业增加值增长10.4%，租赁和商
务服务业增长12.3%，新经济板块对GDP形成结构性拉动。
宏观：按兵不动背后是再加息风险

### Page 4

p 4
要 点
三是政府托底效应显现。专项债发行三季度冲刺，政府债券融资支撑社融；扣除房地产开发后的固定资产投资下降 4.2%，好于整体投资-7.2%的降幅，信息传输业
投资增长30.8%，知识产权产品投资增长9.3%，两重两新项目和新基建对投资形成一定托底。整体看，前三季度经济实现目标主要靠外需和新动能“两条腿”支撑，传统
地产链和消费仍在收缩，经济修复的结构性矛盾突出，总量改善的基础尚不牢固。
续展望来看，四季度外需若欧美需求延续修复 、高端制造出海趋势不变，出口有望维持韧性，但高基数下增速可能边际回落；内需方面 ，政府债券发行高峰已过 ，
后续需观察基建落地效果与企业中长期贷款的可持续性 ，居民信用修复仍依赖地产与消费政策的进一步加码。物价层面，PPI大概率在四季度高位见顶回落，CPI将维持
温和运行，通胀暂不构成政策约束，但输入性成本压力仍需关注。政策端，总量降准降息的紧迫性与空间有限，政策重心将继续偏向结构性工具与扩内需、稳预期的产
业及地产政策，着力提振私人部门信心与内生融资需求。

二十届五中全会于10月26日—29日召开，十四届全国人大常委会第二十五次会议于11月2日—6日召开。人大常委会层面重点关注金融工作报告会否释放货币政策、
地产风险处置的边际表述，以及可再生能源法修订对新能源产业链的长期制度影响
下周重点关注：五中全会、十四届全国人大常委会（十一月初），美联储、欧央行、日本央行公布利率决议

GDP:不变价:当季同比
初步核算，前三季度国内生产总值按不变价格计算同比增长 4.6%。分季度看，一季度增长 5.0%，二季度增长 4.3%，三季度增长 4.5%，三
季度较二季度回升 0.2 个百分点，环比增长 1.2%。分产业看，第一产业增长 3.7%，第二产业增长 3.8%，第三产业增长 5.2%，第三产业继续是经
济增长的主要拉动力。对照政府工作报告全年 4.5%—5% 的预期增长目标，前三季度 4.6% 的累计增速已位于目标区间内。从季度节奏看，三季

度经济在出口和工业生产带动下出现边际企稳，但内需不足、房地产深度调整等制约因素仍在，四季度要争取更好结果仍需宏观政策继续加力。

### Page 12

p 12
工业生产增速继续回升，出口交货值增速加快至17%
图表：工业增加值和出口交货值同比（单位：%）
资料来源：Wind，光大期货研究所
9月规模以上工业增加值同比增长 5.5%，较上月加快 0.3个百分点，环比增长 0.61%；1—9 月累计增长 5.3%。分三大门类看，采矿业增加值
由降转增，同比增长 1.3%；制造业增长 6.0%，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应业增长 5.6%。新动能支撑持续，装备制造业 1—9 月增加值增
长 10.2%，高技术制造业 9 月增长 15.3%、1—9 月增长 14.3%；3D 打印设备、锂离子电池、工业机器人产量 1—9 月分别增长 62.5%、39.5%、
29.1%。外需大幅走高，9 月工业企业出口交货值同比名义增长 17.0%，增速较 8 月的 11.1% 明显加快，1—9 月累计增长 10.7%。但传统行业仍
偏弱，钢材产量下降 3.8%，水泥下降 5.3%，原油加工量下降 8.4%，非金属矿物制品业下降 2.4%，新旧动能分化延续。

1—9月全国固定资产投资（不含农户）334115亿元，同比下降7.2%；9 月环比增长0.07%，由负转正。分领域看，房地产开发投资下降
20.6%，制造业投资下降 2.1%，基础设施投资下降 4.2%，传统投资动能整体仍弱。投资内部新动能保持增长，高技术产业投资同比增长
4.9%，其中信息服务业投资增长 20.2%，航空、航天器及设备制造业投资增长 12.5%，电子及通信设备制造业投资增长 7.6%。但民间固定
资产投资下降10.4%，降幅较1—8 月的10.1% 继续扩大，投资内生动力仍显不足，新动能量级尚不足以完全对冲传统投资下行。

1—9月新建商品房销售面积同比下降13.2%，降幅较1—8月的12.1% 扩大1.1个百分点；二手房交易保持活跃，1—9月二手房交易网签面
积同比增长10.7%，二手房在整个商品房成交中的占比持续保持在50%以上，市场结构继续向存量房倾斜。但新房销售降幅再度扩大，反映
居民购房意愿仍偏弱，新房去化压力仍然较大，房地产销售端尚未出现趋势性企稳。

1—9月全国房地产开发投资 同比下降20.6%，降幅较1—8月的19.9% 扩大0.7个百分点。房地产开发投资、销售和新开工均延续同比下降，
行业整体仍处于调整阶段。房企资金来源和拿地开工意愿仍然偏弱，短期投资下行压力仍大。尽管二手房成交保持活跃、一二手房合计网
签量近期边际改善，但商品住宅开发投资尚未出现企稳信号，政策效果传导至投资端仍需时日。

1—9月基础设施投资同比下降4.2%，降幅较1—8月的4.0% 微扩0.2个百分点。分行业看，新基建及交通枢纽领域保持较快增长，信息传
输业投资增长30.8%，航空运输业投资增长13.8%，水上运输业投资增长13.5%。但传统基建领域仍承压，电力、热力、燃气及水生产和供应
业投资下降6.8%，水利、环境和公共设施管理等领域投资偏弱。整体看，基建投资呈现“新基建强、传统基建弱”的结构性分化。

制造业投资降幅收窄至2.1%，装备制造与高技术领域保持增长
图表：制造业投资同比（单位：%）

其他运输设备制造业投资增长15.8%，但汽车制造业投资下降 6.3%，食品制造业下降 10.3%，化学原料和化学制品制造业下降 7.5%，传统

业投资企稳尚需等待外需和地产链需求回暖。

## Document 599: 宏观经济周报：大宗消费更趋谨慎

Source PDF: all report pdf/宏观经济周报：大宗消费更趋谨慎.pdf
Report source: 国信证券
Date: 2026-10-25
Pages: 14
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

S0980513100001
基础数据
固定资产投资累计同比 -7.20
社零总额当月同比 1.10
出口当月同比 36.30
M2 7.20
市场走势
资料来源：Wind、国信证券经济研究所整理
相关研究报告
《多资产周报-通胀数据偏鹰，美债收益率仍下行》 ——
2026-10-17
《宏观经济周报-AI推动服务供给提质》 ——2026-10-17
《多资产周报-实际利率高企下的黄金反弹》 ——2026-10-11
《宏观经济周报-地产对冲政策试水》 ——2026-10-04
《多资产周报-全球股市显著分化》 ——2026-10-04
生产端景气分化。地产链生产景气回落，纺服和包装材料生产同
步走弱，基建和机电产品生产有所修复。整体来看，工业生产尚
未形成全面回升，地产链库存继续下降，但更多反映供给收缩，
需求修复仍待观察；机电产品库存明显上升，制造业供需差异仍
然突出。
消费需求加速磨底，大宗消费修复动能明显不足。市内出行和线
上物流活跃度均弱于季节性，假期促销效应消退后，汽车销量同

比降幅显著扩大，大宗消费延续走弱。电影票房环比继续回落，
但同比基本持平，服务消费整体表现相对稳定。总体来看，居民
消费呈现商品偏弱、服务相对平稳的特征，内生需求仍待进一步
激活。
房地产成交继续回升，但市场仍处调整阶段。新房和二手房成交
环比上行，库存有所回落，土地成交规模也明显改善，但房价及
二手房挂牌量价指标仍然偏弱。政策端延续“存量时代”主线，
城市更新、老旧住房改造和现房销售制度持续推进，购房贷款贴
息及地方补贴政策进一步落地，地产修复仍需观察政策向销售和
价格端的传导效果。
外贸发运温和修复，运价和航运风险分化。港口货物及集装箱吞
吐量结束连续回落，但反弹幅度有限；出口集装箱运价重新下行，
欧美航线运力明显增加，中东印巴航线持续收缩。霍尔木兹海峡
风险再度上升，能源进口、油轮保险及化工原料成本仍可能承压。
财政收支改善但增量政策必要性下降。9 月广义财政收入和支出
增速差明显收窄，财政力度持续回升，叠加 5500 亿元地方债结存
限额下达，四季度支出有望继续提速，但在全年目标压力可控的

情况下，进一步出台大规模增量政策的必要性有限。货币方面，
资金面延续偏松，DR007 和同业存单利率处于同期低位，央行酝
酿将主要公开市场操作逐步转向隔夜，资金利率中枢或进一步与
短端政策利率锚定。
风险提示：海外市场动荡，存在不确定性。
2026年10月25日

### Page 4

周度观察：大宗消费更趋谨慎
生产端景气分化。地产链生产景气回落，纺服和包装材料生产同步走弱，
基建和机电产品生产有所修复。整体来看，工业生产尚未形成全面回升，
地产链库存继续下降，但更多反映供给收缩，需求修复仍待观察；机电产
品库存明显上升，制造业供需差异仍然突出。
消费需求加速磨底，大宗消费修复动能明显不足。市内出行和线上物流活
跃度均弱于季节性，假期促销效应消退后，汽车销量同比降幅显著扩大，
大宗消费延续走弱。电影票房环比继续回落，但同比基本持平，服务消费
整体表现相对稳定。总体来看，居民消费呈现商品偏弱、服务相对平稳的
特征，内生需求仍待进一步激活。
房地产成交继续回升，但市场仍处调整阶段。新房和二手房成交环比上行，
库存有所回落，土地成交规模也明显改善，但房价及二手房挂牌量价指标
仍然偏弱。政策端延续“存量时代”主线，城市更新、老旧住房改造和现
房销售制度持续推进，购房贷款贴息及地方补贴政策进一步落地，地产修
复仍需观察政策向销售和价格端的传导效果。
外贸发运温和修复，运价和航运风险分化。港口货物及集装箱吞吐量结束

连续回落，但反弹幅度有限；出口集装箱运价重新下行，欧美航线运力明
显增加，中东印巴航线持续收缩。霍尔木兹海峡风险再度上升，能源进口、
油轮保险及化工原料成本仍可能承压。
财政收支改善但增量政策必要性下降。9 月广义财政收入和支出增速差明
显收窄，财政力度持续回升，叠加 5500 亿元地方债结存限额下达，四季
度支出有望继续提速，但在全年目标压力可控的情况下，进一步出台大规
模增量政策的必要性有限。货币方面，资金面延续偏松，DR007 和同业存
单利率处于同期低位，央行酝酿将主要公开市场操作逐步转向隔夜，资金
利率中枢或进一步与短端政策利率锚定。
生产：景气有所分化
本周国内工业品生产景气呈分化态势。高频数据显示，本周房地产投资、
纺织服装和包装材料相关领域景气回落，基建投资、机电产品生产相关领
域景气回升。
具体来看，与房地产投资相关的螺纹钢产量同比降幅再度走扩，库存量同
比延续下行，生产景气度回落、企业被动去库；与基建投资相关的中厚板
产量与上周基本持平，石油沥青开工率同比降幅显著收窄，生产活跃度回

升；与机电产品生产相关的冷轧板卷产量同比降幅收窄，库存量同比显著
上升，生产景气度有所修复；与纺织服装和包装材料领域生产相关的 PTA
开工率同比延续下行，库存天数同比降幅延续收窄，生产景气度回落。

消费：大宗需求显著走弱
市内基础出行回升动能减弱。从一线城市地铁流量观察，本周（10 月 16 日-10
月 22日）日均流量约 4057.82万人次，环比回升 1.6%，弱于去年同期（+2.2%）；
同比回升 1.4%，较上周（+2.0%）走弱，显示居民日常出行需求有所回落。
线上消费活跃度弱于季节性。反映商业活跃度和居民消费的物流高频数据显示，
最新一周（10 月 12日-10月 18 日）邮政快递投递量环比回升 6.6%，相较去年同
期（+14.8%）表现偏弱；同比下降 2.0%，相较上周（+5.5%）显著回落。
从具体消费品类看：假期促销效应消退后，汽车等大宗消费品需求显著回落，电

影等文娱服务消费整体保持平稳。商品消费方面，最新一周（10 月 10 日-10 月
16 日）乘用车销量环比回落 25.4%，显著弱于去年（+8.2%）同期表现；同比下降
34.2%，相较上周（-4.6%）大幅回落，年初以来累计较去年同期下降 18.7%，显
示当前汽车消费修复动能明显不足。服务消费方面，本周（10 月 17 日-10 月 23
日）日均电影票房约 0.3亿元，环比回落 22.8%；同比增长 0.3%，优于去年同期
（-40.1%）表现，主因去年同期受前年高基数影响基数偏低。

房地产：成交继续回升
成交量方面，房地产周度成交均上行。30 个大中城市商品房本周日均成交
套数环比略微上行，当周同比增速-26.3%，低于 2019 年来同期；18 城二
手房成交环比季节性上行，当周同比增速为 13.5%，高于去年同期水平。
库存回落，十大城市存销比为 101.6，前值为 104.8，低于去年同期水平。
土地成交规模环比上升。从 100 个大中城市土地成交数据来看，本期土地
成交数量为 579 宗，环比上行，当周同比增速为 24.0%，累计同比-8.6%。
本期楼面均价和土地溢价率均环比下行。
价格层面，高频数据仍然偏弱。四大一线城市中原报价指数的均值边际下
行，与此不同的是，9 月统计局一线城市二手房价指数环比增速有一定反
弹。此外，二手房挂牌量价指数均边际继续下行。
本周地产政策延续“存量时代”主线。中央层面，10 月 23 日住建部等六

部门印发指导意见，推动老旧街区、老旧厂区功能转换与业态升级，住建
部同期发布《存量时代：房地产高质量发展的新起点》，城市更新成为稳

进出口：港口吞吐回升，中东航线承压
港口吞吐温和回升，节后发运继续修复。本周中国港口货物吞吐量为 2.62亿吨，
环比增长 0.50%；集装箱吞吐量为 696.0 万 TEU，环比增长 1.09%。两项指标结束
此前连续两周回落，反弹幅度有限，外贸发运恢复仍较温和。

综合运价回落，即期运价基本持平。本周中国出口集装箱运价指数（CCFI）降至
1876.27，环比下降 2.41%，结束前一周的小幅修复。综合运价有所承压，而即期
订舱价格仍处高位，运价尚未形成一致的趋势性变化。
航线运力显著回升，但中东印巴延续收缩。本周七条主要航线合计投放 200.55
万 TEU，环比增长 10.4%。美西、美东和欧洲分别增长 39.3%、22.2%和 31.6%，非

### Page 8

洲增长 27.0%；东南亚下降 6.5%，中东印巴下降 23.9%。运力增量主要集中于欧
美航线，中东相关航线已连续两周回落。
中东局势再度升级，霍尔木兹航运风险上升。本周伊朗称 7艘油轮在海峡受损，
10 月商业船只遇袭报告数升至 3月以来最高；伊朗仍保留重开海峡方案，但美伊
核心分歧未解。对我国外贸而言，压力主要体现为能源进口、保险和化工原料成
本上升，并可能压缩中东印巴航线运力。
